## Deferred from: code review of 3-0-adopt-flatbuffers-ffi-transport (2026-06-15)

- **`verify-codegen` is single-file-hardcoded while codegen is glob-driven** [`Makefile.toml`] — generation loops `for fbs in schemas/*.fbs`, but `verify-codegen` only drift-checks `envelope_generated.rs` + `envelope_zanshin.ffi_generated.dart`. A second schema's generated files won't be drift-checked. Deferred: the `verify-codegen` gate is itself WIP-deferred; only bites when `graph.fbs`/`diff.fbs` land — generalise `verify-codegen` to glob then.
- **`cbindgen.toml` `[export] exclude` list is hand-maintained per symbol** [`cbindgen.toml`] — new schemas generating fresh `VT_*`/`ENUM_*` consts will leak into `zanshin_ffi.h` until manually excluded. Deferred: surfaces as visible header churn at codegen/review (not a runtime bug); revisit when the second schema lands. **Evidence added 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-h-request-kind-tag):** the leak also DELETES. cbindgen emits one constant per BARE associated name, so adding `kind` to the 15 request tables displaced `FileChange_VT_KIND` from `zanshin_ffi.h` and `bindings.dart` — only `ChangeIdList_VT_KIND` survives, and 14 of the 15 tagged tables get no `_VT_KIND` at all. Nothing consumes them (grepped across `packages/zanshin_core` and `app`), so this stays deferred, but the fix is now clearly "exclude the flatbuffers `VT_*`/`ENUM_*` constants wholesale", not "add the next symbol to the exclude list". **Evidence added 2026-09-06 (spec-epic-9-followup-i-interdiff-revision-ref, commit `d427eee`):** it happened again on a different name. Adding `table ChangeIdRef { id: string; }` and `table CommitIdRef { id: string; }` displaced `Change_VT_ID` from BOTH `zanshin_ffi.h` and `bindings.dart` in favour of `ChangeIdRef_VT_ID` (same bare associated name `VT_ID`, first table wins), and `CommitIdRef_VT_ID` was never emitted at all — so the header now carries exactly one of the three tables' `VT_ID`, and it is not the one anybody would guess.
- **`max_width` doc/config divergence** [`rustfmt.toml`] — `project-context.md` states `max_width = 100`, but `rustfmt.toml` has long set `120` (pre-existing; not changed by this story). Deferred, pre-existing: reconcile the authoritative value (doc vs. config) separately.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-1-ffi-core-envelope-error-codes-panic-catch (2026-06-15)

- **`FlatBuffers` verifier does not enforce the `ok==false ⇒ error present` invariant** [`schemas/envelope.fbs`, `crates/zanshin-ffi/src/envelope_generated.rs`] — `code`/`context` and the `error` table are all non-`required`, so `root_with_opts::<Envelope>` passes a malformed error envelope with `ok==false` and `error==None`; the Dart-side `env.error() == None`-on-failure case is then an unhandled path. Deferred, pre-existing: this is a 3-0 schema defense-in-depth gap, not a 3-1 defect — the 3-1 builders always populate `error` on the error path. Revisit when the schema is next touched (mark `error` required for the `ok==false` shape, or add a reader-side guard).

## Deferred from: code review of 3-2-handle-module-ffi-strings-zanshin-ffi-version (2026-06-15)

- **`build.rs` git-SHA stamp goes stale under git worktrees / shallow clones** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/build.rs:20`] — `cargo:rerun-if-changed=../../.git/logs/HEAD` assumes `.git` is a directory two levels up. In a linked worktree (or submodule) `.git` is a *file* and the reflog lives under the common dir; shallow/fresh clones may lack `.git/logs/HEAD`. In both cases the rerun trigger watches a nonexistent path, so the SHA stamp never refreshes when HEAD moves. The spec Dev Notes consciously chose simplicity ("do not over-engineer ref-walking"; a stale stamp "is fine for a build-stamp"). Low-impact (cosmetic build-stamp in WIP). A robust fix derives the path via `git rev-parse --git-path logs/HEAD`. Revisit at release phase or if a worktree workflow is adopted.
- **`build.rs` embeds an unrelated parent-repo SHA instead of `unknown`** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/build.rs:5`] — when the source tree has no own `.git` but sits inside another git checkout, `git rev-parse HEAD` succeeds against the enclosing repo, so a source-tarball-in-a-repo build stamps the wrong SHA rather than the intended `unknown`. Contrived; low impact for a WIP build-stamp.

- **One invalid directive discards the whole `ZANSHIN_LOG`, silently downgrading to `info`** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/tracing_init.rs:67`] — `EnvFilter::try_new` is all-or-nothing, so `info,foo=bogus` silently falls back to bare `info`. AC-3.7 only specifies the fully-unparseable case; partial directives are unspecified. `parse_lossy` would preserve valid directives but adds per-directive stderr (worsens the release-stderr decision item). Hardening: `eprintln!`-once on `Err` in debug. Deferred: low / unlikely in the single-cdylib worker isolate.
- **Post-init runtime durability — rollover-into-deleted-dir / disk-full silently drop logs** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/tracing_init.rs:45`] — UTC midnight rollover into a deleted dir, an unmounted volume, or a full disk silently drops subsequent lines, and `tracing-appender` `eprintln!`s on rollover failure (another release-stderr path). No `tracing::*` call sites exist yet (infra-only). Ties to the deferred size/age-cap supportability hardening.
- **Global-init path untested by design** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/tracing_init.rs:84-172`] — tests use scoped subscribers only (AC-8.17) and skip the optional global smoke (T3.4), which is exactly why the two HIGH findings weren't caught at test time. The Patch-1 regression test (unwritable-dir no-panic) partially closes this; a fuller global/FS integration test stays deferred per AC-8.17/T3.4.

## Deferred from: architecture discussion — logging sink (2026-06-18, Winston)

- **OSLog sink via `tracing-oslog` not adopted in WIP** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/tracing_init.rs`] — A colleague suggested macOS Unified Logging (`oslog` / `tracing-oslog`). It is the macOS-idiomatic sink (Console.app + `log stream`, subsystem filtering) and composes as an *additional* `tracing` layer without touching any call site or the FFI — `zanshin_log` and every `tracing::*` site are sink-agnostic. Deferred to release-phase supportability hardening (alongside the size/age-cap rotation) for three reasons: (1) the plain `~/Library/Logs/Zanshin/*.log` file is far easier for a user to attach to a bug report than an OSLog `.logarchive`/sysdiagnose export; (2) `log show` drops `info`/`debug` by default (memory-only unless `sudo log config … persist` is set), so OSLog is a poor *sole* sink for after-the-fact diagnostics; (3) OSLog does not write to stderr, so it would not surface in the `flutter run`/IDE console — the current debug stderr layer does (verified 2026-06-18: `flutter run` forwards the app's native stderr to the IDE console). Its strongest future payoff is `zanshin-mcp`: OSLog is a clean non-stdio sink that satisfies AR-P-5-MCP natively. Revisit at release as an *added* layer (keep the file as source of truth), verify the crate against the pinned Rust toolchain + current macOS, and record an ADR (touches AR-P-5). Crate: `tracing-oslog` (@Absolucy, permissive zlib/MIT-style license).

- **Console log layer is UNCONDITIONAL during WIP dev — re-gate before ship** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/tracing_init.rs`, `crates/zanshin-ffi/Cargo.toml`] — Root cause of the "I see no logs" saga (2026-06-18): the cdylib is built in the `release` profile even under `flutter run`, so the old `cfg!(debug_assertions)`-gated stderr layer was compiled out. Fix made the console (stderr) layer **always on at `trace`** and **removed `release_max_level_info`** so `debug`/`trace` compile in — `flutter run` forwards native stderr, so this is the live dev view (Dart logs arrive via `zanshin_log` too). This DEVIATES from AR-P-5 ("no console output in release builds"): a shipped, Finder-launched app would also emit to stderr (→ Console.app/unified log), and `debug`/`trace` now ship. **Before release:** gate the console layer behind an env var (e.g. presence of `ZANSHIN_LOG`, or a dedicated `ZANSHIN_CONSOLE`), reinstate the `info`-default discipline for the shipped app, and reconsider re-adding `release_max_level_info`. Deferred per Ralph (2026-06-18): during development he does not want to wonder why no logs appear, so console stays unconditional for now.

- **✅ DECIDED 2026-06-21 — App Sandbox DISABLED (ship unsandboxed).** `com.apple.security.app-sandbox` set to `<false/>` in BOTH `app/macos/Runner/DebugProfile.entitlements` and `Release.entitlements`. The analysis below was already pointing here; Ralph made the call. Consequence: `$HOME` is the real home, so the tracing file sink + the diagnostics `log_path` (Story 3.8) now use the literal `~/Library/Logs/Zanshin/` — which **matches** AR-P-5 / PRD / epics / project-context, so **no planning-doc edits are needed**. The now-orphaned `~/Library/Containers/de.dasralph.zanshin/` may be deleted. Hardened Runtime (required for notarization) stays a separate release-phase concern, unaffected by this flip.

  **Original analysis (kept for the record):** App-Sandbox vs. log path + repo filesystem access [`app/macos/Runner/*.entitlements`, `crates/zanshin-ffi/src/tracing_init.rs`] — The Flutter macOS template enables `com.apple.security.app-sandbox`, which redirects `$HOME`, so the log file lands at `~/Library/Containers/de.dasralph.zanshin/Data/Library/Logs/Zanshin/` rather than the `~/Library/Logs/Zanshin/` named in AR-P-5 (verified 2026-06-18). This is correct sandbox behaviour, not a bug. The sandbox is **mandatory for App Store** distribution but **optional for direct distribution** (Developer ID + notarization — Zanshin's planned GitHub Releases / Sparkle path). For a jj/git GUI that must open arbitrary repos anywhere on disk, the sandbox is a significant constraint (needs `files.user-selected.read-write` + security-scoped bookmarks); the likely outcome is **shipping unsandboxed via Developer ID**, after which `~/Library/Logs/Zanshin/` is literal and AR-P-5 needs no change. Decide sandbox yes/no at release; if it stays on, update AR-P-5's path wording to the container location. Logging is only one symptom — the real driver is repo filesystem access.
  - **Mac App Store is effectively ruled out — decisive blocker is `zanshin-mcp` (verified 2026-06-18).** MAS *requires* the sandbox, and a sandboxed MAS app (a) cannot install a CLI into the user's `PATH` and (b) cannot have its binaries launched by *other, unrelated* apps — an embedded helper uses the inherited sandbox (`com.apple.security.inherit`) and runs only as a child of the host app, never standalone from Terminal or another process. Zanshin's MCP server is started by external MCP hosts (Claude Desktop & co.) over stdio and discovered via PATH/config, which is fundamentally incompatible with that model. Secondary frictions: arbitrary-repo access is limited to user-selected paths + security-scoped bookmarks; `jj-lib`'s git backend may shell out to the `git` CLI for some ops (fetch/push), which the sandbox blocks; Watchman (external binary) likewise (degrades gracefully). Net: the "sandbox yes/no" question collapses to **ship unsandboxed via Developer ID + notarization (GitHub Releases / Sparkle)** — the already-planned path. Consistent with most serious git GUIs (Tower, Fork, Sourcetree, GitHub Desktop) all distributing direct, not via MAS. A reduced MAS variant *without* MCP is theoretically possible but not worth the double maintenance for a solo project.

## Resolved during forensic investigation — log path & test isolation (2026-06-21)

- **✅ FIXED — test-isolation leak: the test suites wrote into the developer's real `~/Library/Logs/Zanshin`** [`Makefile.toml` `[tasks.test-flutter]` / `[tasks.test-cargo]`, `crates/zanshin-ffi/src/tracing_init.rs`] — `flutter test` boots a real `ZanshinWorker`, whose isolate entry calls `initLogForwarding` → `zanshin_logging_init` → `init_tracing`, which opens the daily file sink. The test suites pinned `JJ_CONFIG`/`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` but **not** `HOME`, and run unsandboxed, so the sink resolved to the dev's real `~/Library/Logs/Zanshin` and accumulated per-run daily files (e.g. `zanshin.log.2026-06-21`, full of `zanshin_worker_test_*` fixture noise — this is what tipped Ralph off). `cargo test` does not trigger tracing init today but was covered defensively. **Fix:** added a `ZANSHIN_LOG_DIR` override to `resolve_log_dir()` (override wins over `$HOME`; empty value = unset) and pointed both test tasks at `${CARGO_MAKE_WORKSPACE_WORKING_DIRECTORY}/target/test-logs` (under the gitignored `target/`). **`HOME`-pinning was rejected** — it would break Flutter's own `$HOME`-based paths (pub cache, SDK config) during `flutter test`. `ZANSHIN_LOG_DIR` is targeted (only the log sink) and doubles as an ops knob (relocate logs without touching `$HOME`). Surfaced by Ralph's observation + a forensic workflow; cross-refs commits 9fa5b96 / d3aa6ae (the 2026-06-18 sandbox-path doc commits).

## Deferred from: story creation of 3-9-dart-worker-isolate-bootstrap (2026-06-21)

- **Rust `zanshin_worker_init` / process-global async event sink → owned by Story 3.6, not stubbed in 3.9** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/` event surface; `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` §Story 3.6] — The 2026-06-15 re-sequencing note suggested Story 3.9 add a no-op `zanshin_worker_init` Rust stub now to "pin the signature". Dropped at 3.9 story-creation (Ralph, "do it right from the start" — no provisional stubs): you cannot finalize the signature of a `NativeCallable` event contract that Story 3.6 actually designs, so pinning it prematurely is the churn it tries to avoid. **Story 3.9 ships pure-Dart** and reserves only the Dart-side event `ReceivePort`/`SendPort` plumbing (no traffic), so 3.6 adds the producer with zero `ZanshinWorker` restructuring. **Story 3.6 now owns end-to-end:** the event sink, `zanshin_worker_init` (registration entry point), the `NativeCallable.listener` contract, and the Dart event-stream wiring — and must reconcile its JSON-era ACs (`*mut c_char` JSON events, `schema_version`, `progress.v1.schema.json`) to FlatBuffers per ADR-0001 at story-creation time. Recorded in epics.md §3.6.

## Deferred from: code review of 3-9-dart-worker-isolate-bootstrap (2026-06-21)

- **No regression test for the pre-handshake worker-death / handshake-timeout path → build in Story 3.6** [`app/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart` — `ZanshinWorker.start`; `epics.md` §Story 3.6] — The 3.9 review patch made `start()` race the worker's handshake against early death (exit/error) **and** a 10s timeout, so a worker that dies before `bootstrapSP.send` fails fast instead of hanging app boot. The fix is currently UNTESTED: 3.9 has no way to make the worker die before the handshake (the entry always sends it; `initLogForwarding`'s only FFI call is internally `try/catch`'d), so there is no fault-injection hook. **Story 3.6** adds the first worker-side init code that can fail (the event-sink registration / `NativeCallable` wiring), giving a real injection point — add a test there that forces a pre-handshake worker failure and asserts `start()` completes with an error (not a hang) and tears down all five ports. Surfaced by Blind + Concurrency hunters; reconciliation was a `patch` in the 3.9 review.
- **No regression test for kill-on-worker-death → build in Story 3.6** [`app/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart` — `_onWorkerDeath`; `epics.md` §Story 3.6] — The 3.9 review patch made `_onWorkerDeath` call `_isolate.kill(priority: Isolate.immediate)` so a death reported via `addErrorListener` (with `errorsAreFatal:false` the isolate does NOT self-terminate) actually reclaims the isolate and its FFI handles. UNTESTED in 3.9 because no worker code path raises an uncaught *async* error (dispatch is synchronous and fully `try/catch`-wrapped). **Story 3.6**'s future-spawning event sink is the first path that can, so add a test there that forces an uncaught async worker error and asserts (a) pending commands fail and (b) the isolate is no longer alive afterwards. Surfaced by Blind + Concurrency hunters; reconciliation was a `patch` in the 3.9 review.

## Deferred from: ADR-0002 / Story 3.11 — repo-state model (2026-06-22)

_NOT "adapt existing code" items — the shipped code already conforms to ADR-0002 (Story 3.11 AC-5, `cargo make test` green; no rip-out). These are forward-tracked **story-authoring** tasks for when their epic is reached, so the now-settled model isn't re-litigated or forgotten._

- **RESOLVED (2026-08-23) — Epic 4 graph stories 4.3 / 4.4 / 4.6 need a create-story REWRITE: materialized lane cache → lazy revset stream** [`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` §Story 4.3/4.4/4.6; `crates/zanshin-graph/`] — ADR-0002 AC-4 (resolved 2026-06-22) decided the commit graph is a **lazy revset stream** (`stream_graph` + `TopoGroupedGraph`, GG model), **no materialized lane cache**: a spike measured the full 10k graph derive at ~3.1 ms and the first render page at ~27 µs (Story 3.11 Dev Agent Record), so re-deriving on op-id advance is trivially cheap and a held cache buys nothing. The epics 4.3 user-story + body ACs (`LaneCache::build`/`rebuild` on open/op_heads, the `cache.rs` materialized structure, 4.4 `render_slice` over a held `Vec`, 4.6 watcher→rebuild+close graph handle) still describe the **superseded** materialized-cache model — flagged in the 4.3 note + the `project-context.md` `overrides:` entry, but NOT yet rewritten. **Follow-up (at Epic 4 create-story):** re-author 4.3 (`cache.rs` → stream/cursor — DONE), 4.4 (`slice.rs` → delivered as collect-all, not a page — DONE), 4.6 (FFI → re-derive on op-id advance; no long-lived graph handle to rebuild/close; no `start`/`count` scalars — the 4.4 API has none); confirm lane-number/color assignment adds only O(1)/node on top of the ~3.1 ms walk. Re-runnable spike: `bench_graph_*` in `crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs` (needs the `large-10k` fixture). **4.3 DELIVERED (2026-08-22)** as the lazy stream: `zanshin_jj::Repo::walk_graph` (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/graph.rs`) + `zanshin_graph::stream_lanes` (`crates/zanshin-graph/src/stream.rs`, not `cache.rs`); `zanshin-graph` now depends on `zanshin-jj`. **4.4 DELIVERED (2026-08-23)** as `zanshin_graph::collect_lanes` (`crates/zanshin-graph/src/slice.rs`): a collecting sink over `stream_lanes`, no paging (`start`/`count`/`limit`/`reversed`), no `LaneCache`/`render_slice`, `Vec<Row>` return; CLI `--limit`/`--reversed` deliberately not passed through (paging/presentation knobs, dropped with windowing — revisit when windowing returns; rationale in `spec-4-4-return-all-lanes-for-revset-api.md` Design Notes). **4.6 DELIVERED (2026-08-23)** as ONE extern fn `zanshin_graph_slice(handle, revset, prioritize)` (`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/graph.rs`) over `collect_lanes`: no graph handle, no `_build`/`_close`/rebuild, no `start`/`count`; the worker does NOT auto-re-derive on `OpHeadsChangedEvent` — the UI re-requests (backend exposes, UI decides). The 4.4-deferred re-derive-after-external-op test now exists at the FFI surface (`crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/graph_ffi.rs::graph_slice_rederives_from_head_so_an_external_commit_appears_on_the_next_call`) via the new jj-lib write helper `test_helpers::add_commit_off_root` (moved from `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/graph.rs`; `test-helpers` is a test-only tool crate like `xtask`, FR-JJ-16 ACL unaffected — `# WHY:` in its `Cargo.toml`). All three Epic-4 rewrites are closed.

- **Multi-repo / project-overview backend has no story yet (the epics are single-repo)** [`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` (Epic 3); `app/lib/src/worker/`; `crates/zanshin-ffi/` watcher surface] — ADR-0002 § Multi-repo + Story 3.11 AC-6 settled the **model**: the app holds **0..N** handles, and the SourceTree-style overview is **tiered** — *registered* (path only) → *summary* (stateless re-resolve, **no** warm handle, refreshed by that project's op-heads watcher; ~1 ms @ 10k) → *active* (warm handle); **one op-heads watcher per observed project**; watcher events carry a **repo/handle correlation id** on the event port. The worker (Story 3.9) already supports N handles (the Model-A `handleId → pointer` table), but the existing stories are single-repo: 3.4a/3.4b open+read one repo, and the watcher (3.5/3.6) is framed per a single `zanshin_repo_open`. **Gap:** no story covers (a) per-project op-heads watchers with a correlation id, (b) the stateless overview-summary query (cheap re-resolve per row, not a warm handle), or (c) the tiered registered/summary/active lifecycle. **Follow-up:** author a "multi-repo session / project overview" backend story **after** the read path (Epic 2 / 3.4b) and the watcher (3.5/3.6) land — it builds on both. The Flutter overview UI is Ralph's separate scope; this story is the backend it calls.

## Deferred from: code review of 2-12-implement-convert-rs-colocated-git-jj-conversion (2026-06-27)

_bmad-code-review (ultracode multi-agent: 9 layers → dedup → per-finding adversarial verify → synthesis, 71 agents). ZERO production correctness/data-loss defects; the no-data-loss invariant (rollback only `.jj/`, `.git/` never written destructively, no working-copy I/O) and the fast gate (`cargo fmt` / `clippy -D warnings` / `cargo test -p zanshin-jj -p zanshin-ffi`) were independently re-verified clean. The items below are real-but-low / approved-default / convention-consistent — not now-blocking._

- **Best-effort rollback can leave a partial `.jj/` → `AlreadyJjRepo` misclassification (blocks retry); doc over-states unconditional rollback** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs:45` (swallowed `remove_dir_all` Result) ↔ `crates/zanshin-jj/src/detect.rs:64`] — `let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(path.join(".jj"))` discards the rollback Result; if rollback itself fails (EACCES / held lock) the partial `.jj/` survives, the caller still gets `ConvertFailed`, and the *next* `convert_colocated` returns `AlreadyJjRepo` (detect checks `.jj`.is_dir() first) — a retryable state becomes a permanent misclassification, while the `# Errors`/variant doc promises *unconditional* rollback. **Mitigated:** mirrors the repo-wide `Repo::clone` best-effort pattern (`repo.rs:245,247`); no data loss (`.git/` untouched; `rm -rf .jj` recovers); `init_external_git` already self-cleans `.jj/` on its own failure, so this only bites a rollback-of-a-rollback. **Follow-up (optional):** soften the doc to "best-effort rollback", or — if a retry-blocking signal is wanted — enrich the `ConvertFailed` reason when `remove_dir_all` itself errs (diverges from the `Repo::clone` convention). Surfaced by Blind + Edge + Safety; both verify lenses confirmed (one argued dismiss as established convention).

- **Post-import failure leaves additive `refs/jj/keep/*` markers in `.git/` (rollback removes only `.jj/`)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs:45` vs the `import_refs`/`import_head` steps] — `import_refs`/`import_head` write `refs/jj/keep/*` GC-keep markers into the on-disk `.git/` store; if a later step fails, convert's rollback removes `.jj/` but leaves those refs. **Benign:** namespace-isolated in jj's reserved `refs/jj/*`, invisible to `git log`/user refs, self-heals (`recreate_no_gc_refs`) on the next jj op; the user-visible AC-5 guarantee (HEAD SHA + `refs/heads`/`refs/tags` unchanged) holds. **Follow-up (optional):** snapshot `refs/jj/*` before `import_refs` and delete additions in the Err arm, or add a post-import-failure test asserting `.git/refs/jj` is empty afterward. Surfaced by Blind Hunter; one verify lens called the residue benign/self-healing (refute), the other confirmed low/defer.

- **Linked git worktree (`.git` is a file) classified `Git`; convert attempts it where the jj CLI refuses** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/detect.rs:67` → `crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs`] — in a linked worktree `.git` is a gitdir-pointer file, which `detect_repo`'s `.exists()` treats as `Git`, so convert proceeds into `init_external_git` on the shared backing store; the shipping jj CLI explicitly refuses ("Cannot create a colocated jj repo inside a Git worktree", `init.rs` @ v0.42.0 `is_linked_git_worktree`). **Decided:** the OQ4-approved default (let `init_external_git` surface its outcome → `ConvertFailed`, never panic; rollback removes `.jj/`) — no data loss (D3: no working-copy I/O; `.git` opened non-destructively), gated behind future FFI (Story 3.4c). **Follow-up (optional):** a characterization test building a real `git worktree add` dir, asserting `Err(ConvertFailed)` + no leftover `.jj/`; a friendlier CLI-style `NotAGitRepo` refusal would need a gix `is_linked_git_worktree` probe (separate scope). Surfaced by Edge + Safety; verified (one lens re-raised the already-answered OQ4).

- **`.git/ untouched` test scopes refs to `refs/heads`+`refs/tags`** [`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/convert.rs` — `convert_git_repo_does_not_modify_git_dir`] — the before/after ref diff is deliberately narrowed (jj legitimately adds `refs/jj/keep/*`) and uses `rev-parse HEAD` (resolves the symref to a SHA). A hypothetical destructive write to `refs/remotes/*`, `refs/notes/*`, `packed-refs`, or a *symbolic-HEAD repoint* to a same-tip would slip past. **No current defect:** convert is non-destructive by construction (read-only `gix::open`, no `export_refs`) and the fixture has no remotes/notes. **Follow-up (optional, latent if the prod path ever gains a write):** also assert `git symbolic-ref HEAD` unchanged, and assert `refs/remotes/*`/`refs/notes/*` unchanged (or filter `show-ref` to exclude `refs/jj/` and assert full equality). Surfaced by Acceptance + Test-quality; verified.

- **`remote_auto_track_bookmarks` hardcoded `HashMap::new()` ignores user auto-track config** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs` — `import_options` block] — the CLI fills this from `remotes.<name>.auto-track-bookmarks` (`load_git_import_options`); with an empty map, imported remote bookmarks are `New` (untracked) rather than `Tracked`. **No data loss:** untracked remote refs are still imported and pin their commits; the only effect is a user with auto-track configured won't get remote bookmarks auto-tracked at convert time (and matching the CLI would also need the `export_refs` convert deliberately skips). **OQ3-documented default**; current fixtures have no remote bookmarks so the path is untested. **Follow-up (optional):** a one-line doc note near `import_options` duplicating OQ3. Surfaced by jj-api lens; verified.

- **Mutation-sensitive test of the post-init rollback line is not deterministically constructible (was the review's one medium finding)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs:45`; `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/convert.rs`] — review finding #1 flagged that the empty-`.git` rollback test is self-cleaned by jj-lib's `init_with_factories` `inspect_err` (`workspace.rs:339`) BEFORE convert's own `remove_dir_all(.jj)` runs, so it does not prove the production rollback line (deleting `convert.rs:45` keeps that test green). **Empirical outcome (2026-06-27):** three principled, source-grounded failure injections that occur AFTER a successful `init_external_git` — a `refs/heads/*` pointing at a missing object; a corrupt loose commit object; a `refs/heads/*` pointing at a tree object — ALL leave `convert_colocated` returning `Ok`. jj-lib's git import degrades gracefully: gix silently skips refs whose target object is missing/unreadable during ref listing, and `import_head` no-ops when `head_id()` can't peel. So the rollback line is correct but its load-bearing window (an import / `tx.commit` failure after init) is reachable only via a genuine I/O fault (e.g. a disk error mid-commit), which needs a fault-injection seam in `convert_into` to simulate; a chmod/permission-based test would be flaky and platform-fragile (the project bans flaky tests). **Follow-up (optional):** if a future write-side story adds a fault-injection seam (cf. the deferred 3.9 worker-death items), add a post-init-failure test asserting `Err(ConvertFailed)` + `!.jj exists` that fails iff `convert.rs:45` is removed. NOTE: the detached-HEAD (AC-2/D3a) and AC-7 detect-error coverage gaps from the same review WERE closed with passing tests this round. Surfaced by Test-quality lens; both verify lenses confirmed; reclassified patch→defer after the empirical injection attempts.

## Deferred from: code review of 2-13-implement-external-git-import-conversion-convert-import (2026-06-27)

_bmad-code-review (ultracode multi-agent: 8 finder lenses → dedup [17 raw → 10 unique] → per-finding adversarial verify from 2 diverse lenses, 29 agents). ZERO production data-loss/correctness defects on the primary two-path flow; the no-data-loss invariant (source never destructively written, rollback removes only the destination) and the jj-lib-0.42 API port (`init_external_git`→`import_refs`→`import_head`→guarded `check_out`→materialise) were independently verified faithful to `do_init`'s External branch. 1 decision-needed + 2 test-only patches handled in the story; the two items below are pre-existing/cosmetic._

- **Test 14's `.jj`-only rollback branch (`created_dir==false`) is mutation-insensitive** [`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/convert.rs` (`convert_import_failure_with_pre_existing_dest_removes_jj_only`); `crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs:143-145`] — same root cause as 2.12's deferred "post-init rollback line not deterministically constructible": no source-grounded failure leaves a `.jj/` on disk at the moment our `else { remove_dir_all(.jj) }` runs (jj-lib's `init_with_factories` `inspect_err` self-cleans `.jj/` on init failure, `workspace.rs:339`), so deleting the entire `else` body keeps all convert tests green (proven by mutation). **No current defect / no data loss:** the *dangerous* mutation — rewriting the arm to `remove_dir_all(workspace_path)`, which would wipe a user's pre-existing destination — IS caught by Test 14's second assertion `assert!(dst.exists())`; only the no-op "delete the whole branch" mutation survives, and removing a rollback when there is nothing to roll back is harmless. Already noted in the story Coverage note (spec line 389). **Follow-up (optional):** if a future write-side story adds a fault-injection seam in `import_into`, add a post-init-failure test that pre-creates a stale `dst/.jj` and asserts our `else` branch removes it. Surfaced by Test-quality lens; one verify lens confirmed (mutation experiment), the other dismissed (dangerous mutation already caught by `dst.exists()`).

- **Nested-destination rollback leaves orphaned empty parent directories** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs:137` (`created_dir`), `:142` (`remove_dir_all(workspace_path)`) vs `:236` (`create_dir_all`)] — when `workspace_path` is nested under parents that don't yet exist (e.g. `<tmp>/a/b/workspace`), `create_dir_all` builds the whole chain but the failure rollback removes only the leaf, leaving empty `a/`, `b/` behind. **Cosmetic, no data loss:** source untouched; leftovers are empty dirs the bridge itself created; all jj-lib writes (`.jj/`, working copy) are under the leaf and ARE cleaned. D7 is verbatim-locked to "remove only the destination" and NFR-R-2 says "rollback removes ONLY the destination", so removing parent dirs would arguably over-reach. The bridge's `create_dir_all` is a documented liberalization (D4 step 1) of the CLI's single-level `fs::create_dir` (which errors on a missing parent, never producing an orphan). **Follow-up (optional):** mirror the CLI with single-level `create_dir`, or capture the first-actually-created ancestor and remove down to it on rollback; add a two-level-nested rollback test. Surfaced by Edge-case Hunter; one verify lens confirmed (nit/patch), the other dismissed (correct-by-spec / D7 locked).

## Deferred from: code review of 3-4c-repo-detect-convert-ffi-wrappers-zanshin-repo-detect-convert (2026-06-28)

_bmad-code-review (ultracode multi-agent: 3 prescribed layers [Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor] + 3 domain lenses [Rust-FFI/FlatBuffers, Dart-seam/worker, anti-pattern-compliance] → dedup [15 raw → 14 unique] → per-finding adversarial verify → completeness critic, 24 agents). **ZERO production correctness / memory-safety / data-integrity defects** — all three domain lenses returned clean, no banned anti-pattern triggered, and the FFI ownership + FlatBuffers wire contract + error taxonomy + worker seam were independently verified sound. 4 findings refuted as false-positives. 1 decision-needed + 6 patches handled in the story; the items below are pre-existing/by-design/cosmetic._

- **`decodeRepoDetectEnvelope` + `…Internal` twin is needless API surface — but it is the repo-wide convention** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/repo_detect.dart:47-50`] — two public functions forward verbatim to the private `_decodeRepoDetectEnvelope`; the worker uses the `Internal` name, the unit test the non-`Internal` name. **Not a 3.4c defect:** `status.dart` / `bookmarks.dart` / `remotes.dart` all define the identical `decodeXEnvelope` + `decodeXEnvelopeInternal` + `_decodeXEnvelope` trio, and the story explicitly mandated "copy 3.4a verbatim". Collapsing the twin would diverge from the established seam and should be a separate repo-wide refactor. **Follow-up (optional):** if the twin is genuinely unwanted, eliminate it across all FFI decoders in one pass. Surfaced by Blind Hunter; verified confirmed (literal duplication) but reclassified to defer (repo-wide convention, out of scope).

- **`RepoDetect.kind` / `suggested_kind` are non-`(required)` → an absent field silently reads as `Jj`** [`schemas/envelope.fbs` (`table RepoDetect`)] — the wire format does not enforce the conceptually-mandatory `kind`; a truncated/buggy producer omitting it is indistinguishable from a genuine `Jj`. **By-design:** this is inherent FlatBuffers scalar/enum default semantics shared by every enum field in the schema (`ChangeKind`, `ConflictState`, …); the producer is trusted in-repo Rust whose builder always writes `kind`; the schema doc already states "kind is always set". Marking it `(required)` would diverge from the schema-wide convention and change generated code. **Follow-up (optional):** a schema-wide policy decision on `(required)` enums, not a 3.4c change. Surfaced by Blind Hunter; verified true-but-minor (nit).

- **detect performs `detect_repo` then `find_repo_root` from the same path (~3 redundant syscalls)** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/repo.rs` (`zanshin_repo_detect`)] — on the `Neither`/`PathNotFound` path, `find_repo_root` re-classifies `start` as its first ancestor, re-running the `try_exists`/`.jj`/`.git` stats already done by `detect_repo`. **By-design:** the cost is one extra level (~3 syscalls), not a doubled traversal; the two-function composition is the documented intentional API split (`detect_repo` = single-dir; `find_repo_root` = walk-up). **Follow-up (optional):** only if a future combined classify+ancestor API lands in `zanshin-jj`. Surfaced by Blind Hunter; verified nit ("leave as-is").

- **`toNativeUtf8()` (malloc) freed with `calloc.free`** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/repo.dart` (`zanshinRepoDetect` / `zanshinRepoConvert`)] — the path CString is allocated with package:ffi's default `malloc` but freed via `calloc.free`. **Cosmetic, safe:** both `malloc.free` and `calloc.free` delegate to the same C `free`, and `calloc.free` is already the pattern used elsewhere in the same file. **Follow-up (optional):** pass `allocator: calloc` to `toNativeUtf8()` (or use `malloc.free`) for read-at-a-glance clarity. Surfaced by Blind Hunter; verified nit.

- **No null-pointer / invalid-UTF-8 test for the two new extern fns** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/repo_ffi.rs`] — all detect/convert tests pass valid UTF-8 via `cstr(...)`; neither new fn is exercised with a null `*const c_char` or non-UTF-8 bytes, despite the docstrings documenting `ffi.null_pointer` / `ffi.utf8_error`. **Low value:** the shared `cstr_to_string` helper is already unit-tested for both error paths (`ffi_strings.rs`), and the wiring (`cstr_to_string(...)?`) is byte-identical to the already-tested `zanshin_repo_open`; only the trivial one-line wiring is uncovered. **Follow-up (optional):** add `zanshin_repo_detect(null)` / non-UTF-8 tests if per-symbol error-branch coverage is wanted. Surfaced by Blind Hunter; verified nit (regression rationale overstated).

## Deferred from: code review of 3-5-watcher-subsystem-with-notify-debouncer-full (2026-06-28)

_bmad-code-review (ultracode multi-agent: 3 prescribed layers [Blind Hunter + Edge Case Hunter + Acceptance Auditor] + 2 domain lenses [Rust-concurrency/FFI, test-quality] → per-finding adversarial verify by two skeptics [correctness + spec-grounding] → conservative dedup/synthesis, 58 agents). 26 raw → 19 survived → 1 decision-needed + 8 patches + 1 defer + 12 dismissed. **No memory-safety or data-loss defect; the watcher's thread/closure Send+'static-ness, panic-free debouncer thread, channel lifecycle, and drop/stop semantics verified sound.** The item below is upstream/near-impossible on the target platform._

- **Non-UTF8 repo path panics inside notify's `append_path`** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/watcher.rs` (`debouncer.watch(...)` in `Watcher::new`) → notify 8.2.0 `fsevent.rs:393` `path.to_str().unwrap()`] — a non-UTF-8 watched path panics inside notify, executed synchronously on the `Watcher::new` **caller** thread (NOT the debouncer background thread), so it does not violate the "never panic on the debouncer thread" rule. **Near-impossible on the target platform:** macOS APFS/HFS+ paths are effectively always valid UTF-8, the jj-managed `.jj/...` segments are ASCII, and `repo_path.canonicalize()` runs first. The project rule forbids `unwrap` on FFI-reachable paths, but this `unwrap` is upstream notify's, not ours. **Follow-up (optional):** pre-validate `repo_path.to_str().is_some()` and return a typed `WatcherError` before calling `watch()`, converting the upstream panic into a typed error. Surfaced by the Rust-concurrency lens; verified nit (caller-thread, target-platform-precluded).

## Deferred from: code review of 3-10-end-to-end-watcher-test-filesystem-mutation-dart-callback (2026-07-04)

- **rawToId-ABA race manifests as a real gate flake under load (first in-the-wild sighting)** [`packages/zanshin_core/test/worker/events_test.dart` (`two repos open — touching one dispatches only its handleId`); race documented under "code review of 3-6" below] — during the 3-10 review's independent `cargo make test` run on a heavily loaded host (44 parallel review agents), this pre-existing 3.6 test failed once: a stray event carried repo B's `handleId` although only repo A was touched. Log evidence matches the documented ABA family: `Worker: event for unknown raw handle 36226958080 — dropped` immediately followed by `Worker: repo opened raw=36226958080 → handleId=0` — a freed `RepoSession` allocation address was reused by a new open while a stale event was still queued in the worker isolate's message queue. 6/6 green re-running the same file on an idle machine; a second independent full-gate run followed. NOT caused by the 3-10 diff (the failure precedes `watcher_e2e_test.dart` in the same run; 3-10 is test-only). **Assessment:** the 3-6 acceptance "benign for coalescing-idempotent watcher events" holds for production semantics, but the two-repos isolation *test* asserts exactly what the accepted race violates — under heavy load it is nondeterministic, which collides with the "a test is deterministic or deleted" rule. **Follow-up:** the correlation-id (generation/session-id) redesign is already scheduled for Story 6-4a; pull it forward — or de-flake/rescope the two-repos test — if the flake recurs at normal load. **RESOLVED same day (Ralph, 2026-07-04):** pulled forward during this review — events now carry a process-unique, never-reused `session_id` (allocated in `arm_session_with`, returned in `RepoHandle { handle, session_id }`, demuxed via the worker's `sessionToId` map); a stale event can only be dropped, never misattributed. See the annotated 3-6 entry below.
