The `reset` needs `start_working_copy_mutation` — the locked-working-copy lifecycle **Story 9.22** owns — and reversing project decision D2 ("Zanshin never snapshots") was not that run's call. Same cause, same owner: on a DIRTY working tree the CLI's colocated `maybe_snapshot` additionally writes a `snapshot working copy` operation and a second `import git refs`, neither of which Zanshin writes, so the colocated op-log witness pins "and nothing more" only for a clean tree. Severity medium. **Follow-up:** land it with Story 9.22, the story that gains the locked-working-copy lifecycle; the divergence is already stated at the function, the module doc, both public verbs' rustdoc, the FFI rustdoc and `docs/ffi-abi.md`, so 9.22 only has to delete those paragraphs as it closes it.

- **The CLI's refusal to colocate inside a linked Git worktree is not ported** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs::convert_colocated`; CLI `cli/src/commands/git/init.rs:209-219` @ v0.44.0] — `jj git init --colocate` over a `.git` that belongs to a linked worktree hard-errors ("Cannot create a colocated jj repo inside a Git worktree.", hinted towards the main repo or `jj workspace add`). `detect_repo` classifies such a directory as `Git` (its `.git` is a gitdir POINTER file), so it reaches `convert_into`, which ACCOMMODATES the shape — reading `is_colocated` rather than refusing — and succeeds where the CLI refuses. Pre-existing since Story 2.12 and outside follow-up K's ledger bullet, but a divergence under Ralph's rule, and untested either way. Severity medium. **Follow-up:** port `is_linked_git_worktree` (three lines of `gix`: `repo.git_dir() != repo.common_dir()`) into `convert_colocated`'s precondition block and surface the refusal through an existing error code, witnessed by a `git worktree add` fixture.

- ~~**No Zanshin verb sets the operation's `workspace_name` metadata**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-07 (spec-epic-9-followup-l-op-workspace-name, commit `98cd53f`)** [every `start_transaction` call site in `crates/zanshin-jj/src`; CLI `cli/src/cli_util.rs::start_repo_transaction` `:2883-2894` @ v0.44.0] — the CLI opens every transaction through a helper that calls `tx.set_workspace_name(workspace_name)`, and jj-lib's `Transaction::set_workspace_name` writes `op_metadata.workspace_name` (`jj-lib-0.44.0/src/transaction.rs:120-122`) — persisted op-log state, not terminal UX, and distinct from the command-arg attributes the same helper sets (those Zanshin deliberately lacks). `grep set_workspace_name crates/` has zero hits, so every Zanshin operation records `workspace_name = None` where a CLI-written one names the workspace. Surfaced by follow-up K's op-log-parity claim but crate-wide and pre-existing. Severity medium. **Follow-up:** set it once in whatever opens transactions for the mutation family (`mutate.rs`) plus the convert verbs' own three sites, witnessed by reading the metadata back off the op store; natural to land with Story 9.21 (`op log`/`op show`), the story that would surface the field. **Resolution:** landed on its own rather than waiting for 9.21, because the metadata is written whether or not anything reads it back. `op_support.rs` gained a fifth `cli_util` port, `start_repo_transaction(repo, workspace_name)`, reproducing the oracle's first two statements — `ReadonlyRepo::start_transaction` and `Transaction::set_workspace_name` — and NOT its third, the `command_args_to_transaction_attribute` loop, which encodes a terminal command line ADR-0003 replaces with a GUI. Nothing else is invented: `hostname`/`username`/`time` already come from jj-lib's own `create_op_metadata` off `UserSettings`, and `is_snapshot` belongs to the CLI's snapshot caller (`cli_util.rs:2099`), which Zanshin has no counterpart for until Story 9.22. All 14 call sites route through it — the seven in `mutate.rs` (which covers `bookmark_ops.rs` and `git.rs` too: all eleven of those verbs funnel through `finish_or_no_op_at` and open no transaction of their own), `op_log.rs::undo`, `remotes.rs::git_fetch`, `repo.rs::clone_into`'s two, and `convert.rs`'s three, the last reached by giving `init_git_refs` and `import_git_head` a `workspace_name` parameter. `clone_into`'s Step-2 transaction is routed too even though it is dropped without committing, so the guard has zero exceptions to encode. **The guard is a unit test, not a lint:** the repo has no `clippy.toml`, and a workspace-root `disallowed-methods` entry would fire in `crates/test-helpers` (12 legitimate direct openers), `crates/xtask` (3) and 14 sites across `crates/zanshin-jj/tests`. The inline scanner in `op_support.rs` reads the crate's own `src/` for the BARE identifier `start_transaction` — unambiguous precisely because the helper is named after the CLI's `start_repo_transaction`, which does not contain that substring, so a UFCS call is caught and a call to the helper never false-positives — and carries three negative probes plus a subdirectory-descent probe. Witnessed behaviourally by `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/op_workspace_name.rs`: eleven rows reading the metadata back off the op store via jj-lib (NOT through `crate::Operation`, which is deliberately not widened — that stays Story 9.21's surface), one per verb family plus two second-workspace scenarios. The second of those exists because nine rows run in a workspace literally called `default` and would pass against a helper that ignored its argument; substituting `WorkspaceName::DEFAULT` at `remotes.rs:260` leaves them green and fails only that row. `convert.rs`'s sites and `clone` CREATE the workspace they name, so no second-workspace row can exist for them. No error code, no `.fbs` edit, no `extern "C"` or public signature change; coverage held at 99.27% with the baseline unmodified. **One divergence at the same surface is NOT closed and is carried below:** the `reconcile divergent operations` operation jj-lib writes from `RepoLoader::load_at_head`.

- **A conversion that fails after the alias step orphans the secure-config directory it created** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs`, both verbs' rollback arms] — `config::create_repo_config_file` calls `SecureConfig::load_config`, which creates `<config_dir>/jj/repos/<id>/` with a `metadata.binpb` and writes `.jj/repo/config-id`; the rollback deletes `.jj/` (2.12 D4 / 2.13's `created_dir` branch) and never reaches outside it, so the directory survives with a metadata back-pointer to a path that no longer exists. The CLI cannot reach this state because it never rolls back at all. Documented in three rustdoc blocks rather than fixed, because extending the rollback outside `.jj/` changes the stated reach of the no-data-loss invariant, which follow-up K's intent froze. Severity low. **Follow-up:** decide whether the rollback may delete the directory it just created (it knows the path) or whether the orphan is acceptable; if the former, witness it in `tests/convert_alias_write_failure.rs`, whose current blocker fails BEFORE the directory exists.

- **The `LazyLock` env-var pin the test suites share is sound only by convention** [`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/{convert,git_remotes,git_sync}.rs`, `crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/repo_ffi.rs`] — each sets `JJ_CONFIG` / `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` / `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` from a `LazyLock` forced inside whichever test body wins the race, while sibling test threads are already running: exactly the hazard Rust 2024's `unsafe env::set_var` signature marks. What makes it work is that every test in those binaries calls `pin_env()` as its first statement, which nothing states or enforces. The pattern predates follow-up K (follow-up J introduced it); K only widened the blast radius, because a test that forgets the call now writes a `trunk()` alias into the developer's real `~/.config`. Severity low. **Follow-up:** move the pin to a process-start hook (a `#[ctor]`-style initialiser or a shared `test-helpers` entry point) so it runs before any test thread, and drop the per-test call.

## Deferred from: spec-epic-9-followup-l-op-workspace-name (2026-09-07)

_The `workspace_name` run closed its ledger bullet in full — one helper, all 14 call sites, a creep guard and eleven witnesses. Its review surfaced one item it did not close: a divergence at the SAME observable field, on a path the run's mechanism structurally cannot reach._

- **The `reconcile divergent operations` operation still records `workspace_name: None`** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src` — every `workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head()` call site; jj-lib `RepoLoader::load_at_head` `jj-lib-0.44.0/src/repo.rs:706-729`; CLI `cli/src/cli_util.rs::resolve_operation` `:773-805` @ v0.44.0] — when a read finds divergent op heads, jj-lib merges them and WRITES an operation described `reconcile divergent operations`. `load_at_head` builds it with a literal `let workspace_name = None;` (`repo.rs:712`), so that operation carries no name. The CLI never calls `load_at_head`: it drives `op_heads_store::resolve_op_heads` itself and passes `Some(workspace_name)` into `RepoLoader::merge_operations` (`cli_util.rs:792-799`), so a CLI-written reconcile operation names the workspace. Same field, same op log, same NFR-R-2 parity claim as follow-up L — but not a `start_transaction` call site and not written by a Zanshin verb, so follow-up L's creep guard is structurally blind to it. Reachable in exactly the shape FR-MCP-9 describes: a GUI, an MCP server and an external `jj` CLI writing the same repo. Severity medium. **Follow-up:** replace `workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head()` with a `zanshin-jj` helper that reproduces the CLI's `resolve_op_heads` + `merge_operations(Some(name), Some("reconcile divergent operations"), [])` shape, then `load_at`. That is 64 call sites across 16 modules — every READ verb, not just the writes — so it is the head-load path (ADR-0002 territory), its own run, with its own creep guard; a witness can fabricate the divergent op heads the way `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/op_log.rs:525` already does.

## Deferred from: spec-promote-windowing-splash-to-main (2026-09-12)

_Promoting the windowing-API POC into the real entrypoint closed its intent in full (single entrypoint, no `--windowing` flag, chrome ported onto the Dart-created window, multi-window API promoted). Its review pass applied nine patches and left three items: one consequence of an explicit owner decision, one latent race in an API that has no caller yet, and one verification obligation that an unattended seat cannot discharge._

- **Closing the last window skips the worker's graceful shutdown** [`app/lib/src/features/window/app_windows.dart::_exitProcess`; Flutter SDK `packages/flutter/lib/src/services/binding.dart:502-507`] — the registry exits with `ServicesBinding.exitApplication(AppExitType.required)`, which the framework documents as exiting "immediately without querying the application", so the `System.requestAppExit` round-trip never runs and `AppLifecycleListener.onExitRequested` — the hook `_WorkerLifecycleListener` uses to call `ZanshinWorker.close()` and release the FFI handles — is never reached. ⌘Q still goes through AppKit and shuts the worker down cleanly, so the two quit paths now differ. Before this change AppKit terminated on the last closed window and that path did reach the hook. The mechanism is an explicit owner decision in the story intent ("the registry lifecycle exits via `ServicesBinding.instance.exitApplication(AppExitType.required)`"), which is why it was not "fixed" under review. Practical harm is low — the OS reclaims the handles at process exit and jj holds no lock outside a transaction — but the asymmetry is real and the comment in `worker_lifecycle_module.dart` now documents it rather than hiding it. Severity medium. **Follow-up (decision, Ralph):** either switch the registry exit to `AppExitType.cancelable` so both paths run the same shutdown, or give `AppWindows` an explicit pre-exit hook that closes the worker before the required exit. Doing nothing is defensible too — but then the divergence should be stated in the design doc as intended, not just as observed.

- **`AppWindows.openOrActivate` is not re-entrant** [`app/lib/src/features/window/app_windows.dart:113`] — the `_entries[key]` lookup happens before the `await create(...)`, and nothing records the in-flight creation, so two calls for the same key that overlap both miss the guard and open two native windows, the first of which is then unreachable by key. There is no production caller today (`ZanshinWindowHost` uses `create` + `register` directly), so it cannot bite until the planned project-list window opens project windows per double-click — precisely the gesture that can fire twice. Severity low. **Follow-up:** add a `Map<String, Future<WindowEntry>>` of pending creations, checked and awaited alongside `_entries`, and witness it with two un-awaited `openOrActivate` calls for one key in `app/test/features/window/app_windows_test.dart`; natural to land with the project-list window story that gains the first caller.

- **The ported chrome's visual result is unverified, and so is closing the last window by hand** [`app/macos/Runner/MainWindowChrome.swift`] — story intent decision 5 says "Verify visually that the result matches today's look". `screencapture` has no screen-recording permission on this machine, and the VM-service inspector screenshot documented in the `project-verify-macos-ui-without-screen-permission` memory note renders the Flutter widget tree, not the AppKit layers the chrome is made of, so it cannot answer the question even in principle. What was verified structurally: a CGWindowList probe of the launched debug bundle shows one on-screen window at 1200×800, origin (264,142), alpha 1.0, layer 0 — and a CG height equal to the requested *content* height only holds under `.fullSizeContentView`, so the chrome demonstrably ran; the saved-frame path, the slow-boot splash and two hot restarts were exercised as well. What remains unseen: vibrancy, the 20 pt corner radius, the gray traffic-light rings and their hover cross-fade, and the close-the-last-window gesture (the exit rule is unit-tested against an injected exit, and `q`-quit was exercised). Severity medium. **Follow-up:** one look from Ralph at a debug run — or grant screen-recording permission once so future runs can settle it from the shell.

## Deferred from: spec-splash-card-from-design (2026-09-12)

_The splash rebuild closed its intent in full (560×320 painted card, live status and version channel, reduce-motion rule, visual check against the design SVG). Its review pass applied ten patches and left two items that are product decisions rather than defects._

- **The painted splash is invisible to assistive technology, and ignores the system text size** [`app/lib/src/features/bootstrap/splash_card.dart`, `app/lib/src/features/bootstrap/splash_card_render.dart`] — the kanji lock-up, the ZANSHIN wordmark and the tagline are canvas paint with no `Semantics` node, so a screen reader gets no app identity from the splash at all; the status line is a plain `RichText` with no `liveRegion: true`, so its changes are never announced; and both footer lines use `RichText` with a fixed 11.5/12 px size, which defaults to `TextScaler.noScaling`. The previous Lottie card had no semantics either, so nothing regressed — but this is the first version of the splash that carries text worth announcing. The card is a fixed-geometry design artifact, so honouring the system text size is not a mechanical change: the footer grid (baseline y = 276, right edge x = 524) would have to give. Severity medium. **Follow-up (decision, Ralph):** decide what the splash should announce (an app-identity label plus a live status region is the cheap version) and whether a scaled text size may reflow the footer, or the whole card should scale with it.

- **Nothing regression-tests the painted design; the check is a one-time screenshot** [`app/lib/src/features/bootstrap/splash_card_render.dart`, `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/design/splash-flutter-screenshot.png`] — roughly 600 of that file's 678 lines (the four gradients, the top light edge, the wave ornament, the grain layer, the watermark, the lock-up, the wordmark path and its gold shader, the tagline, the divider, the rail and the runner, the inner border) carry no assertion. The design was verified once, by eye, against `design/assets/zanshin-splash.svg`, and that capture is committed — evidence, not a gate. A mistranscribed constant from here on ships green. The repo has no golden-test infrastructure at all, and goldens for a card with three custom fonts and a soft-light blend are exactly the fragile kind, which is why this was not "fixed" under review. Severity medium. **Follow-up (decision, Ralph):** either accept eyeball verification for the splash and say so, or stand up golden tests once — in which case this card is a good first subject, with the grain absent and the ticker frozen so the image is deterministic.

## Deferred from: code review of macos-folder-drop (2026-09-14)

Plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-13-macos-folder-drop.md`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-13-macos-folder-drop-design.md` (commits 186f065..7d33453).

- **A removed `FolderDropView` keeps its native drag session** [`packages/zanshin_shell_macos/macos/zanshin_shell_macos/Sources/zanshin_shell_macos/FolderDropHost.swift`, `FolderDropView.swift`] — `detach` removes the view but not its `session`; if a view's last target unmounts and remounts during a live drag, a late `draggingExited`/`draggingEnded` on the removed view sends `dragExited(viewId)` and ends the new Dart visit for the same view id. Rare. Fix: clear `session` without sending when the view leaves its window (`viewDidMoveToWindow` with `window == nil`); fold in the next item.
- **`draggingEntered` overwrites an open native session without `dragExited`** [`FolderDropView.swift`] — Dart's `_enter` already closes a stale session and AppKit sent exit/ended in the POC (checklist rows 9, 15); calling `endSession()` first would keep the guarantee inside the view.
- **Non-`PlatformException` errors in the `zanshin_shell/folder_drop` handler are silent** [`packages/zanshin_shell_macos/lib/zanshin_shell_macos.dart`] — `MethodChannel` turns them into a `code: 'error'` reply without reporting, so a decoder `TypeError` or a dispatcher bug looks like "drops silently rejected". Callbacks are already wrapped. Fix: catch, `FlutterError.reportError`, rethrow.
- **A non-String element in `paths` replies `error` instead of `bad-args`** [`zanshin_shell_macos.dart` decoder, `List<String>.unmodifiable`] — native only sends strings; both codes reject natively.
- **A failed `enableFolderDrops` is not retried** [`packages/zanshin_shell/lib/src/folder_drop_dispatcher.dart`] — enabling happens only on a view's 0→1 target count; `attach` only fails on wiring errors, which are reported. Revisit if `unknown-view` ever shows up — that window would stay without drops.
- **Dispatcher tidy-ups** [`folder_drop_dispatcher.dart`] — `_sessions.clear()` in `release` never has anything to clear; `_call` repeats `_report`'s `FlutterErrorDetails` block (only the context string differs). Do it the next time the file is touched.
- **Redundant generation counter** [`FolderDropView.swift` `Session`] — `Session` is a class held by the reply closure, so an identity check would do.
- **Untested dispatcher clauses** [`packages/zanshin_shell/test/folder_drop_target_test.dart`] — an update without a session after exit/drop, a drop whose paths differ from the enter's, returning to a target starting a new visit, the view's last target unmounting mid-drag, and a failing `disableFolderDrops`. Each follows from a tested invariant; add the "new visit" test when the project overview builds hover UI on it.
- **Swift drag session and folder filter have no automated tests** [`packages/zanshin_shell_macos/macos/zanshin_shell_macos/Sources/zanshin_shell_macos/`] — owner's decision (no Swift runner in the gate); verified by a throwaway `swiftc` fixture check (script in the plan, Task 5) and manual Finder drags. After any Swift edit re-run the check and checklist rows 2, 9, 14, 15.
- **Runtime checks not run yet** — hot restart (plan Task 8 row 17) and a drop target inside the top titlebar strip (spec assumption 2; app windows use `fullSizeContentView` with a toolbar and `DragInterceptorView` above the content view there). Run both before wiring drops into the real project overview.
- **Hover during a Finder drag is sporadic with a late exit** — engine behavior found in the POC (spec assumption 4 refuted); hover styling must come from `FolderDropTarget` callbacks. The spec's runtime checklist item "Whether `MouseRegion` reacts during the drag" is now answered and could be reworded.
- **Demo only: project rows call `toggleNode` on tap** [`app/lib/main3.dart`] — no-op for childless nodes; the spec says clicking a group toggles it.

## Surfaced 2026-09-17 while authoring Stories 9.22 / 9.26 (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph asked why `jj diff --summary` printed "Done importing changes from the underlying Git repo." and whether Zanshin gets that behaviour from jj-lib. It does not: the import and the snapshot are CLI orchestration (`cli_util.rs::snapshot_impl` @ v0.44.0), jj-lib triggers none of it, and jj has no file watcher by default. That became Story 9.26 (spec authored, `spec-9-26-implicit-snapshot-colocated-git-import.md`) with 9.22 refined as its prerequisite (`spec-9-22-file-track-untrack-locked-wc.md`); the two D2 ledger items above were moved into 9.26. Reading the settings path for 9.26 surfaced one further, previously unrecorded parity gap._

- ~~**`Repo::open`/`init`/`clone` open the workspace with a hardcoded `UserSettings` stub, not the user's jj config**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-23 (spec-9-27-effective-user-settings-config-stack, commit `a3d4d91e2c34666960751a1aa35720ada7e9c66a`): `make_settings` is gone; open/init/clone/convert build `UserSettings` from the CLI's full stack (System/EnvBase/User/Repo/Workspace/EnvOverrides, `whoami` hostname/username), re-derived before every mutation on a stack change; tests pin identity through `JJ_USER`/`JJ_EMAIL`/`JJ_OP_*` and the per-test stubs are retired. **MOVED 2026-09-17 → Story 9.27** (`spec-9-27-effective-user-settings-config-stack.md`, same session: Ralph asked for the values to be read from jj's config AND to be editable from Zanshin at repo and global scope, SourceTree-style; the reading half is 9.27, the editing half is **Story 9.28**, `spec-9-28-config-surface-list-get-set-unset-path-edit.md`. Note: jj reads `user.name`/`user.email` from its OWN config only — there is no `.gitconfig` fallback to port). Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::make_settings` (`:1729-1746`); every `Workspace::load`, `start_repo_transaction` and commit write downstream of it; contrast `config.rs::load_for_workspace`, which builds the EFFECTIVE stack but only for named reads] — `make_settings` is `StackedConfig::with_defaults()` plus one `ConfigSource::User` layer setting `user.name = "Zanshin"`, `user.email = "zanshin@localhost"`, `operation.username = "zanshin"`, `operation.hostname = "localhost"`; no user config file, no `JJ_CONFIG`, no repo or workspace layer. Consequences, all on-disk and CLI-visible: (1) every operation Zanshin writes carries `username = zanshin`, `hostname = localhost` in `jj op log`, and every commit Zanshin creates or rewrites takes its author/committer signature from that stub (`Zanshin <zanshin@localhost>`) where the CLI writes the user's `user.name`/`user.email` — the most visible divergence a user alternating between CLI and Zanshin can hit; (2) jj-lib reads `fsmonitor.backend`, `snapshot.*` and every other `UserSettings`-backed knob from the stub, so a user's Watchman configuration is silently ignored and Story 9.26's precondition cannot be CLI parity until this is fixed; (3) 20+ integration tests re-create the same stub in their own setup (the `zanshin@localhost` blocks in `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/*.rs`), pinning the current identity as expected behaviour. Never recorded before. Severity **high** under Ralph's rule. **Follow-up (recommend its own short story, landed BEFORE 9.26):** build `UserSettings` in `Repo::open`/`init`/`clone`/`convert` from the effective stack (`config::load_for_workspace` → `UserSettings::from_config`; `init`/`clone` need the pre-workspace variant `clone_into` already uses), keep test identity deterministic through an explicit `JJ_CONFIG` layer the way `tests/clone_config.rs` and `config_user_layer.rs` already do, and witness it by reading op metadata and a fresh commit's signature back off the store; then retire the per-test stubs.

## Surfaced 2026-09-20 while reviewing bookmark sync state (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph pasted a `jj bookmark list --all-remotes` from another project and asked whether Zanshin knows a bookmark's local/remote state, whether local and remote are in sync, the CLI's `(ahead by N commits, behind by M commits)`, and how a Fetch button could be enabled and carry a SourceTree-style count. Three findings, each recorded as a story the same day so they are not lost:_

- **The shipped `Bookmark` payload cannot answer "in sync"** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/bookmarks.rs` (`Bookmark { name, target: ChangeId, kind }`; untracked remotes skipped at `:97`), `schemas/envelope.fbs:485`, `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/bookmarks.dart:54`] — `target` is a change id, so after a local rewrite the local bookmark and `<name>@origin` compare EQUAL while `jj bookmark list` reports ahead 1 / behind 1 (same change, different commits — the pasted feature-bookmark row); the graph rows' `CommitRef.synced` (4.8) is a bool and omits synced remote rows by design. The CLI's distances are a LOCAL computation (`cli/src/commit_templater.rs:1689-1716` @ v0.44.0: `jj_lib::revset::walk_revs` + `count_estimate` over the last-fetched target, synced fast path), so Zanshin can reproduce them without network. → **Story 9.29** (`bookmark list --all-remotes` parity: `synced`/`tracked`/`conflicted`, ahead/behind, untracked + deleted rows).
- **`git fetch` has no default-remote resolution** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs:684`, `Repo::git_fetch(remote_name, ..)`] — one explicit remote, always `glob("*")`; the CLI's `get_default_fetch_remotes` (`git.fetch` → the single remote → `origin`), `--remote`/`--all-remotes`/`--tracked`/`--branch`/`--tag`, per-remote `fetch-bookmarks`/`fetch-tags`, the multi-remote single transaction, `remotes.<name>.auto-track-bookmarks` and the `No git remotes to fetch from` refusal (= the Fetch button's enable rule) are all missing (`cli/src/commands/git/fetch.rs` @ v0.44.0). → **Story 9.30**.
- **Story 9.24 (remote-change poll, FR-JJ-13c) was in `epics.md` but missing from `sprint-status.yaml`** since 2026-09-12 — added 2026-09-20. It is the pre-fetch "something changed on origin" signal (no commit count, by design); 9.29 gives the post-fetch commit distances, 9.30 the enable rule.

## Surfaced 2026-09-21 while designing commit drag-and-drop (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph asked which jj verbs make sense when change S is dragged onto change T (or into the gap above/below T), how the UI can tell up front what is possible, and whether the CLI's own precondition code can be reused. Verified @ v0.44.0: the drop position IS the CLI's `-o`/`-A`/`-B` location, one shared helper (`cli_util.rs:3551` `compute_commit_location`) serves `rebase`/`new`/`duplicate`/`revert`/`split`/`squash`, and Story 9.1 had already ported that helper plus `check_rewritable`/`ensure_no_commit_loop` into `op_support.rs` (tested, unwired). That became **Story 9.31** (`drop_actions`, the read-only capability query; mirrored into `sprint-status.yaml` the same day). Two further items, recorded so they are not lost:_

- **`jj rebase -s`/`-b` with `-A`/`-B` is not refused up front when the target lies inside the moved set** [`cli/src/commands/rebase.rs:499-508`, `:560-569` @ v0.44.0 — `check_rebase_destinations` ("Cannot rebase X onto descendant Y") runs only under `rebase_destination.onto.is_some()`; `compute_commit_location` checks only the gap (immutable children, loop between the gap's parents and children)] — `jj rebase -s S -A T` with `T ∈ S::` reaches jj-lib's `compute_move_commits` (`lib/src/rewrite.rs:619`) unchecked; whether that refuses, no-ops or relocates is undetermined here. **Follow-up (9.16):** pin the corner by test and make 9.31's preview mirror the verb's actual answer; until then the UI treats "S with descendants" dropped into a gap inside S's own descendants as not offered.
- **Precondition-phase contract for the Tier C verbs** [`sprint-status.yaml` 9-9 / 9-15 / 9-16 / 9-18, dated NOTEs] — each verb's precondition phase is ONE pure fn (the CLI's `plan_*` split) called by the verb AND by `drop_actions`; whichever story lands first creates it. Recorded here because a verb built without that split would force 9.31 to duplicate its rules and drift from them.

## Surfaced 2026-09-23 while implementing Story 9.27 (effective config stack)

_Implementing `spec-9-27-effective-user-settings-config-stack.md` surfaced items outside its scope or deliberately left as they are:_

- ~~**Read verbs keep the settings of the last derive**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-23 (spec-9-26-implicit-snapshot-colocated-git-import, commit fadbbfc199a40e3b67bac72acc3e4b7dd8160732): every snapshotting read is now `&mut self` and runs `refresh_settings` first inside `Repo::precondition`. [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::refresh_settings`, called only by the 16 `&mut self` verbs] — per the spec's Design Notes no read verb became `&mut self`: a read re-resolves the effective config per call for everything it reads by NAME (aliases, `mine()`'s `user.email`, `ui.conflict-marker-style`, the log defaults), but jj-lib-internal settings a read consults through the loaded `Workspace` (e.g. `merge.*` during a tree merge) stay those of the last open/mutation until the next mutation re-derives them. Story 9.26 decides its receivers and runs its snapshot after `refresh_settings`; if a read ever needs the refreshed settings, that is where it becomes `&mut self`.
- ~~**A wrong-typed working-copy / git key now refuses `Repo::open`, where it used to refuse only the verb that read it**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-23 (spec-9-28-config-surface-list-get-set-unset-path-edit, commit `fe54820ceadb4c8c167f4211e7c8a336928e358a`): the nine config verbs are handle-less free functions over a workspace ROOT (`zanshin_jj::config_*`, `zanshin_config_*`), so they run in exactly that state — as the CLI's config commands do, which never load the workspace — and repair it; witnessed by `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/config_cmd.rs::a_wrong_typed_git_key_blocks_open_but_not_the_config_verbs` (`Repo::open` refuses, `config_unset(Repo, …)` succeeds, `Repo::open` then succeeds). Original entry: [`config.rs::load_config_fault`, tests `git_sync.rs`, `op_support.rs`, `file_ffi.rs`, `git_ffi.rs`, `repo_write_ffi.rs` re-ordered to open BEFORE breaking the stack] — CLI parity (every CLI command in such a repo fails at start-up, because jj-lib reads `git.*` / `ui.conflict-marker-style` / `working-copy.*` / `fsmonitor.*` while loading the workspace), reported as `jj.config_error` naming the key. Observable change for the GUI: a repo with such a config no longer opens at all until the user fixes it — Story 9.28's config surface is the way out, and it must be reachable WITHOUT an open handle for that reason.
- **`Repo::init` with a failing git backend leaves `.jj/` behind** [`repo.rs::init`, pre-existing; now reachable with a wrong-typed `git.*` key in the user config, which refuses as `RepoError::Config` from inside `init_internal_git`] — `clone` and the convert verbs roll their `.jj/` back, `init` never did. Whether the CLI's `jj git init` leaves it too is unverified; Story 9.20 (init/clone destination parity) owns it.
- **`pubspec.lock` drifts on every `dart`/`flutter` resolve** (`vector_math` 2.4.2 → 2.4.3) — unrelated to 9.27, reverted from its diff; the lock wants a deliberate `pub upgrade` commit.
- **`Repo::git_fetch` and `Repo::describe` write an operation when nothing changed** — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.40** (`9-40-no-change-verbs-write-no-operation`; the gate goes into `mutate::finish_verb`). [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs::git_fetch`, `crates/zanshin-jj/src/mutate.rs::describe`; pre-existing, found by the 9.27 review] — the CLI's `jj git fetch` / `jj describe` end in `tx.finish` (`cli/src/commands/git/fetch.rs:254`, `cli/src/commands/describe.rs:262` @ v0.44.0), which prints "Nothing changed." and commits NO operation when `!tx.repo().has_changes()` (`cli/src/cli_util.rs:2790-2795`) — and therefore shows no identity warning. Zanshin commits unconditionally, so `jj op log` gains an entry the CLI would not write, and `MutationResult.identity_missing` (Story 9.27, set iff an operation was committed) fires with it. Op-log parity defect; `git_fetch` is reworked by Story 9.30, `describe` needs its own no-change check.
- ~~**A Finder/Dock-launched Zanshin does not see the user's shell environment**~~ — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.36** (`9-36-finder-launch-login-shell-environment`; **decided 2026-09-24, Ralph: option (a)** — resolve the login-shell env once at start-up). **RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.36):** `app/lib/main.dart::_startApp` calls `ZanshinWorker.adoptLoginShellEnvironment()` before the app exists; Rust (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/environment.rs`) runs `$SHELL -l -i -c` once, reads its `env -0` dump under a timeout and adopts it write-once — `ConfigEnv::from_env`, `detect.rs`'s `HOME`, `wc_ops.rs`'s `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and the fetch/clone `git` subprocess environment read that map; a failed read keeps the started-with environment and warns `env.login_shell_unresolved`. Witnessed by `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/login_shell_environment.rs`. **REDIRECT 2026-09-25 (Ralph):** Dart now reads the shell and Rust only adopts the map — `ZanshinBootSequence` runs the injected `adoptShellEnvironment` step alongside the settings load; `zanshin_core`'s `adoptLoginShellEnvironment()` reads `$SHELL -l -i -c` detached and hands the variables to `zanshin_environment_adopt` → `zanshin_jj::adopt_environment` on the main isolate; the warning is built in Dart. Remaining gaps: the Story 9.36 section below. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/config.rs::ConfigEnv::from_env`; found by the 9.27 review] — the stack reads `JJ_CONFIG`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `JJ_USER`/`JJ_EMAIL`/`JJ_OP_*`, `VISUAL`/`EDITOR`, `NO_COLOR` exactly as the CLI does, but a GUI app launched by launchd gets a minimal environment, not the login shell's. A user who sets `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `JJ_CONFIG` in a shell rc then gets a different config (and identity) in Zanshin than in the terminal `jj`. Product decision for the app bootstrap (e.g. import the login-shell environment once at start-up); not a stack defect.
- ~~**Three `app/` splash-card layout tests fail at the pre-9.27 baseline**~~ **RESOLVED 2026-09-26 (console session, at Ralph's request):** the TEST was stale, not the code — Ralph's commit `a50688f` ("update Splash Screen", 2026-09-13) deliberately re-gridded the card from 560×320 to 620×400 (`_statusRight` 524→584, `_footerBaseline` 276→370, `_dotY` 272→366, version/status 12→13 px) and left the test's oracle constants on the old grid; `zanshin_bootstrap_test.dart` now asserts the new grid (no assertion loosened), full `app` suite green. Open for Ralph: `design/assets/SPLASH-SPEC.md` + `zanshin-splash.svg` still describe 560×320. Original entry: [`app/test/features/bootstrap/zanshin_bootstrap_test.dart`: "an empty status and an unknown version still paint the card", "the splash card lays its footer out on the design grid", "the status line follows its listenable and stays right-aligned" — Expected 524.0, Actual 584.0] — verified identical with every 9.27 change stashed at `677dbf546fc2d19203019b06b6ea1ef431aa64fd`; no `app/` file is touched by 9.27. Keeps `cargo make test` red at `test-flutter` (every other gate step green). UI is Ralph's scope.

## Surfaced 2026-09-23 while implementing Story 9.28 (config surface)

_Implementing `spec-9-28-config-surface-list-get-set-unset-path-edit.md` surfaced items outside its scope or deliberately left as they are:_

- ~~**Read-side secure-config fix-ups are still not run**~~ — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.37** (`9-37-secure-config-fixups-every-command`; direction: the CLI's `maybe_load_config` at every command start, reversing "Reading never writes" per the parity rule). **RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.37):** `config.rs::SecureLayer::fix_up` calls jj-lib's own `maybe_load_config` for the repo, then the workspace layer, in the CLI's start-up order (inside `load_raw`, after the system and user layers) at every CLI-command start Zanshin plays — `Repo::open_with_report` (and `Repo::open`), the settings refresh every precondition-running verb starts with, and the `config_*` verbs given a workspace root (the `edit` halves: the fix-up alone); each jj-lib message becomes `jj.config_migrated` / `jj.config_copied` / `jj.config_regenerated` (kind from the id file's state), carried on success AND failure (`CreationOutcome` for open, the new `ConfigOutcome<T>` for the config verbs, `OpenedRepoHandle.warnings` in Dart). Witnessed by `crates/zanshin-jj/tests/secure_config_fixups{,_user}.rs`, `crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/secure_config_ffi.rs`, `packages/zanshin_core/test/worker/secure_config_test.dart`. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/config.rs::secure_config_path`; CLI `cli/src/cli_util.rs:4545-4549` @ v0.44.0 → `SecureConfig::maybe_load_config`] — the CLI migrates a legacy `.jj/repo/config.toml` (and re-points / copies a moved or copied repo's config) at the start of EVERY command; Zanshin's read path — every verb, `config_list`/`config_get` included — deliberately never writes (`config.rs` module doc "Reading never writes"), so `config_list` shows the legacy path until any CLI run or a Zanshin config WRITE (which goes through `SecureConfig::load_config` and so migrates) moves it. Pre-existing documented stance across every verb; a product decision for Ralph. Severity low.
- **A broken `conf.d` user file is named by its DIRECTORY, not the file** [`config.rs::add_path_layers` → `file_error(path, …)` with the `load_dir` path; pre-existing] — the start-up refusal of the config verbs (and every other verb) reads `…/conf.d: Configuration cannot be parsed as TOML document: TOML parse error at line 1 …`; the CLI names the file itself (`ConfigLoadError::Parse.source_path`). The GUI still finds the file (the editor halves list every `conf.d` file as a candidate), but the `context` is less precise than the CLI's hint. Fix: prefer `source_path` from the parse error when present.
- ~~**`zanshin_config_set`'s precondition is dropped on the Dart ERROR path**~~ — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.35** (`9-35-dart-error-results-keep-precondition-warnings`, generalised to every command's error result, precondition + 9.32 warnings). **RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.35):** `ZanshinFfiException` carries the error envelope's `precondition` and `warnings`, filled by the one shared `precondition.dart::decodeEnvelopeErrorInternal` that every `!env.ok` branch in `packages/zanshin_core` throws — `configSet`'s included; witnessed by `test/worker/error_reports_test.dart::a_failed_identity_set_keeps_its_check_report`. [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/config.dart::decodeConfigSetEnvelopeInternal`] — Rust stamps `Envelope.precondition` on the error envelope too (a `user.name` write whose identity snapshot ran, then `jj.config_would_overwrite`), but the Dart decoder throws `ZanshinFfiException` and `ZanshinWorker.send` records preconditions only for HANDLE-scoped commands. Harmless today (the only reachable combination is an identity key over a table), and the snapshot's effect is visible on the next handle verb anyway; widen when the UI needs it.
- **Remote writers keep the CLI's `existing_repo_config_file` rule, not `edit_config_file`** [`config.rs::remove_remote_from_repo_config` / `rename_remote_in_repo_config`] — noted for the record, not a defect: the CLI's `jj git remote remove/rename` resolve their file through `existing_repo_config_file` (no file → no-op, nothing created), whereas `jj config set/unset` use `edit_config_file` (create-forced). Story 9.28 moved all writers onto the shared `load_config_file`/`save_config_file`/`SecureLayer` helpers but kept each writer's CLI selection rule, so observable behaviour is unchanged.

## Surfaced 2026-09-23 while implementing Story 9.22 (file track / untrack)

_Implementing `spec-9-22-file-track-untrack-locked-wc.md` surfaced items outside its scope or deliberately left as they are:_

- ~~**After a `file_track` that tracked a new path, every later working-copy-touching Zanshin verb fails until something snapshots — Story 9.26 MUST land before `file_track` is wired into the UI**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-23 (spec-9-26-implicit-snapshot-colocated-git-import, commit fadbbfc199a40e3b67bac72acc3e4b7dd8160732): the pre-verb snapshot runs before `lock_working_copy` and every `finish_transaction`, and `file_track` merges its stats; the pins flipped to `tests/file_track_untrack.rs::working_copy_verbs_after_track_snapshot_the_tracked_path_into_at_first` and the worker test `verbs_right_after_track_succeed_and_see_the_tracked_path`. [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/mutate.rs::lock_working_copy` / `sync_working_copy`; pinned by `tests/file_track_untrack.rs::a_second_verb_after_track_needs_the_next_snapshot_first` and the `zanshin_core` worker test `a_second_verb_right_after_track_surfaces_jj_concurrent_working_copy`] — the CLI's `track` discards its snapshot tree (GH #8298), so the tracked path lives only in the working-copy STATE, whose tree then differs from `@`'s. The CLI never notices, because its NEXT command snapshots first and moves the path into `@`. Zanshin has no pre-verb snapshot yet (the contract's Never: that is 9.26), so: (a) another `file_track`/`file_untrack` refuses with `jj.concurrent_working_copy` (the CLI's own "Concurrent working copy operation" check) and writes nothing; (b) WORSE, every verb that moves `@` through `mutate::sync_working_copy` (`describe`/`new_change`/`edit`/`abandon`/`restore`/`absorb`/`undo`/bookmark and git verbs that move `@`) COMMITS its operation and only then fails in `Workspace::check_out` with jj-lib's `ConcurrentCheckout` (`jj.jj_lib_error`), leaving the working copy STALE (its recorded op is behind the head) — recoverable today only with the CLI's `jj workspace update-stale` (Zanshin's is Story 9.23). Not a product decision: the fix is fully determined — **Story 9.26**'s per-verb snapshot, run BEFORE `lock_working_copy` for the two tracking verbs and before every `workspace_helper` verb, puts the tracked path into `@` first, exactly as the CLI does. Documented on `Repo::file_track` and `RepoHandle.fileTrack`.
- **Shared `op_support::build_matcher` still refuses a path entry with a trailing `/` (and `./`, `//`, in-workspace absolute paths) where the CLI normalises it** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_support.rs::build_matcher`, used by `restore`/`absorb` (9.3) and the four file reads (9.4)] — the CLI parses a bare path argument with `RepoPathUiConverter::parse_file_path` (cwd-relative, normalising); Zanshin's shared helper takes jj's internal string form verbatim, so `jj restore dir/` works while `Repo::restore(paths=["dir/"])` is `jj.invalid_input`. Story 9.22's two verbs already parse like the CLI with cwd = the workspace root (`wc_ops::parse_paths`, `RepoPathBuf::parse_fs_path(root, root, entry)`; the matrix row `paths=["nope/"]` requires it); the six older verbs were not touched (out of this story's scope). Pre-existing parity gap. **Follow-up:** move `build_matcher` onto the same parse (it then needs the workspace root) and update its `paths = [""]` / refusal docs + the pinned `build_matcher_treats_an_empty_path_entry_as_the_repo_root` test.
- **No `--at-op` / `--ignore-working-copy` refusal** (`check_working_copy_writable`, `cli_util.rs:1271-1284` @ v0.44.0) — Zanshin has neither global flag, so the CLI's "This command must be able to update the working copy." has nothing to refuse; not ported.

## Surfaced 2026-09-23 while implementing Story 9.26 (implicit snapshot + colocated Git import)

- ~~**Wrong-typed snapshot knob is refused BEFORE the head import; the CLI refuses AFTER it — deferred decision for Ralph**~~ — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.34** (`9-34-snapshot-knobs-after-git-head-import`; direction: the CLI's order). **RESOLVED 2026-09-24 (Story 9.34):** `Knobs` is gone; the precondition builds only the start-up environment (`snapshot::CommandEnv`: fileset/revset alias tables, `immutable_heads()` parse) before the lock, and `snapshot_working_copy` reads `snapshot.auto-track`, the ignore files and `snapshot.max-new-file-size` itself, after `import git head` — the double fault now writes `import git head` then fails, as the CLI does (twin-checked against jj 0.45.1). Pins: `tests/snapshot_colocated.rs::a_wrong_typed_knob_fails_after_the_head_import_like_the_cli`, `a_wrong_typed_knob_is_refused_only_once_the_held_lock_is_free`, `a_bad_immutable_heads_alias_is_refused_before_the_lock`. [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/snapshot.rs::Knobs::read`, `repo.rs::Repo::precondition`; CLI `cli_util.rs::workspace_helper_with_stats` → `snapshot_impl` → `snapshot_working_copy` @ v0.44.0] — the spec's matrix row "Wrong-typed knob: refused before the lock" wins, so Zanshin reads the snapshot knobs (`snapshot.auto-track`, `snapshot.max-new-file-size`, the ignore files) and `immutable_heads()` before taking `git_import_export.lock`; the Git import options are read inside the refs step, as the CLI does (Story 9.26 follow-up review). The CLI reads the snapshot knobs only inside `snapshot_working_copy`, i.e. AFTER its lock and its `import git head`. Observable only in the double fault "Git HEAD moved outside AND a wrong-typed snapshot knob": the CLI writes `import git head` and then fails, Zanshin writes nothing and fails. Pinned by `tests/snapshot_colocated.rs::a_wrong_typed_knob_is_refused_before_the_lock`. **Decision needed:** keep the matrix's ordering, or read the snapshot knobs after the head import like the CLI (the lock-wait witness would then need a different shape).
- **`snapshot.auto-update-stale = true` still refuses a stale working copy** [read and type-checked only on the stale error path in `repo.rs::Repo::precondition`; nothing branches on it] — its `true` arm is the CLI's `recover_stale_working_copy_impl`, which is Story 9.23's. Pinned by `tests/snapshot_precondition.rs::auto_update_stale_still_refuses_until_the_recovery_lands`; 9.23 flips that pin.
- **The cost of the per-verb precondition on a GUI refresh is unmeasured (unverified, medium if true)** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::Repo::precondition`; found by the 9.26 review] — every snapshotting verb now reloads the effective config, re-reads the ignore files, resolves the knobs and (without Watchman) scans the whole working copy, exactly like one CLI command does; but one GUI refresh fans out into several verbs, so it scans several times. The intent forbids caching/debouncing (the UI decides when to call), and Story 2.4's "< 100 ms on the large fixture" status criterion was not re-measured. **Follow-up:** benchmark `status()` + `walk_graph` on the large fixture with the precondition (`benches/`), then decide whether the UI must batch its reads or call `snapshot()` once per refresh.
  - *2026-09-30, Story 4.10 — the graph half is measured* (`crates/zanshin-ffi/benches/graph_topology.rs`, medians): `Repo::snapshot()` on an unchanged tree costs ~1.7 ms on `medium` and ~2.5 ms on `large-10k` (loose and packed) — the fixtures' working copies are tiny, so this is the fixed per-verb share (config load, ignore files, a small scan) — out of 5 ms (`medium`) / 22–26 ms (`large-10k`) for the whole `graph_topology(all())` call. The graph view's refresh now runs ONE precondition (the topology call); its content pages run none. A real working copy scans longer (28 ms on the 1.4k-commit repo measured 2026-09-26); the `status()` half stays unmeasured.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 — Story 9.26 follow-up review

_Follow-up review of `spec-9-26-implicit-snapshot-colocated-git-import.md` (commits fadbbfc + c073636, verified against HEAD after 9.28 and the 9.27/9.22 follow-ups). Patched in the same pass: the Git import options are read inside the precondition's `import git refs` step where the CLI reads them (a malformed value now leaves `import git head` / `snapshot working copy` in the op log, as the CLI does), `git_import_options` type-checks all four `GitSettings` keys, plus wire/ok-arm/`git_refs_rebased` witnesses and doc fixes. The precondition's own op-log parity was re-checked against v0.44.0 and found equal. One item needs a decision; the rest are PRE-EXISTING op-log divergences in the finishing verbs, found by the pass's CLI-parity layer (three of them were already tracked and are cross-referenced, not duplicated in substance):_

- ~~**Convert discards its snapshot report — decision for Ralph**~~ — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.33** (`9-33-creation-paths-report-identity-snapshot`; Ralph: extend the return value, via Story 9.32's warning channel). **RESOLVED 2026-09-24 (Story 9.33):** `convert_colocated`/`convert_import` return a `CreationOutcome` whose `precondition` is `maybe_snapshot`'s report (`Some` iff it started, partial on failure) and whose warnings carry the snapshot's own plus its stats warnings; `zanshin_repo_convert` stamps both on its ok and error envelopes, Dart `convert` returns a `CreatedRepoHandle` with `precondition` / `warnings`. [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs::maybe_snapshot`; FFI `zanshin_repo_convert`] — the conversion snapshot's facts (files left untracked by `snapshot.max-new-file-size` / `auto-track`, the colocated `.jjconflict` warning, export / HEAD-reset failures) are dropped; AC-3 keeps `precondition` off convert envelopes. The CLI's `maybe_snapshot` in `jj git init` over an existing repo prints them. Surfacing them needs a field on the handle-producing results (FFI + Dart) — the same surface question as the 9.27 follow-up's `identity_missing` item below. Severity low.
- **`edit` of the current `@` writes an operation** — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.38** (`9-38-edit-current-at-is-noop`). [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/mutate.rs::edit`; CLI `cli/src/commands/edit.rs` @ v0.44.0] — the CLI checks `get_wc_commit_id() == Some(new_commit.id())`, prints "Already editing that commit." and opens no transaction; Zanshin commits `edit commit <id>` (and, for a discardable `@`, jj-lib may abandon/recreate it). Pre-existing (no guard before 9.26). Severity medium. **Fix:** the same early return before the transaction, returning the no-op result.
- **`undo` is the v0.42.0 single-step walk-back, not v0.44.0's undo stack, and writes an op when nothing changes** — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.39** (`9-39-undo-stack-v044`, the stack walk; `redo` stays Story 9.21's) **and Story 9.40** (the no-change op, via `finish_verb`'s gate). [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_log.rs::undo`; CLI `cli/src/commands/undo.rs` @ v0.44.0] — v0.44.0: undoing an undo-operation undoes the operation that undo restored to (a second `jj undo` goes further back; redo is the separate `jj redo`), a target that is itself an undo resolves to the original operation, and `tx.finish` writes nothing when the restored view equals the current one (e.g. undoing a pure `git_refs`/`git_head` op). Zanshin's doc states the second undo redoes the first, and it always commits. Pre-existing. Severity medium. **Fix:** port the `UNDO_OP_DESC_PREFIX` stack walk (and decide whether `redo` becomes a verb).
- **`git_fetch` description uses the raw remote name** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs::git_fetch`; CLI `cli/src/commands/git/fetch.rs:258` @ v0.44.0 formats it with `as_symbol()`] — names that are not plain revset identifiers are quoted in the CLI's op description. Pre-existing; belongs with Story 9.30, which also owns the refspec defaults ("`git fetch` has no default-remote resolution" above). Severity low. The no-change fetch op is the existing "`Repo::git_fetch` and `Repo::describe` write an operation when nothing changed" item; note for its fix: `mutate::finish_verb` is the port of `WorkspaceCommandTransaction::finish` (`cli_util.rs:2790-2806`) MINUS its `has_changes()` early return — adding the gate there fixes the no-change op of fetch and undo at once; `describe` (unchanged-description filter, `describe.rs:219-226`) and `edit` (above) need their own guards.
- **Re-found, already tracked:** the linked-worktree convert refusal ("The CLI's refusal to colocate inside a linked Git worktree is not ported") and the unnamed `reconcile divergent operations` op ("…still records `workspace_name: None`") — both pre-existing, unchanged by 9.26.

## Surfaced 2026-09-23 — Story 9.27 follow-up review

_Follow-up review of `spec-9-27-effective-user-settings-config-stack.md` (commits a3d4d91 + fc362ce, verified against HEAD after 9.22/9.26/9.28). Patched in the same pass: creation paths (`init`/`clone`/both converts) now resolve the stack for the NEW workspace's paths like the CLI's `settings_for_new_workspace`, so a `--when.repositories` per-directory identity signs what the creation writes. One item needs a decision:_

- ~~**Clone / init / convert never report `identity_missing`, where the CLI warns**~~ — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.33** (`9-33-creation-paths-report-identity-snapshot`; Ralph chose option (a), extend the return value, realised through Story 9.32's warning channel). **RESOLVED 2026-09-24 (Story 9.33):** `jj.identity_missing` rides the creation outcome exactly where the CLI's flow finishes a transaction — clone once (its single `clone` op; the CLI's up-to-three count is a recorded 9.20 deferral), `convert_import`'s `import git head` finish, the conversion snapshot's own finishes; never `init` or a raw commit. FFI `zanshin_repo_init`/`_clone`/`_convert` carry it in `Envelope.warnings`, Dart `initRepo`/`cloneRepo`/`convert` as `CreatedRepoHandle.warnings`. [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::Repo::clone` / `Repo::init`, `crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs::convert_colocated` / `convert_import`; FFI `zanshin_repo_clone` / `_init` / `_convert` return only a `RepoHandle`] — the CLI's identity warning lives in `finish_transaction`, which `jj git clone` reaches via `tx.finish(ui, "fetch from git remote into empty repo")` (`cli/src/commands/git/clone.rs:457` @ v0.44.0, also `:314`, `:352`) and `jj git init` on an existing Git repo via `tx.finish(ui, "import git head")` (`init.rs:268`). With an empty identity, a GUI clone (the typical first action on a new machine) writes its operations and `@` commit with the empty identity and gives the UI no typed fact, so the "set your identity" prompt only appears on the first later mutation. Fix options: (a) new surface — an `identity_missing` field on the handle-producing results (FFI + Dart change, a new decision on the envelope shape); (b) none in the backend — the UI calls Story 9.28's `config_get user.name/user.email` after a clone/init/convert. Needs Ralph's call; severity medium.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 while triaging the 9.22–9.28 follow-up decisions (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph's decisions on the open items of the 9.22/9.26/9.27/9.28 runs and follow-up reviews. Each item became a story (epics.md 9.32–9.40, rows in `sprint-status.yaml`); the originating bullets above carry a **MOVED 2026-09-24** marker._

- **Warnings become a coded channel, not one-off fields** → **Story 9.32** (`9-32-warning-channel-every-envelope`). Ralph asked for an HTTP-like status with a code the UI can act on (missing identity → offer to configure it) instead of parsing a description. Decided: `Envelope.warnings: [Warning { code, message }]` on ok and error envelopes — a LIST, because several warnings hold at once — with codes in the error registry, one code per CLI `ui.warning_*` site on ported paths; `ui.status` lines stay typed facts, hints are not ported. `MutationResult.identity_missing`, `Precondition.working_copy_immutable_new_commit` and `.jjconflict_files_present` fold into it. Prerequisite of 9.33 and 9.35.
- **Creation paths report identity and the conversion snapshot** → **Story 9.33** — Ralph: "extend the return value" for both the 9.27 follow-up's `identity_missing` item and the 9.26 follow-up's convert-snapshot-report item.
- **Snapshot knobs in the CLI's order** → **Story 9.34**; **Dart error results keep precondition + warnings** → **Story 9.35**; **Finder/Dock launch environment** → **Story 9.36** (Ralph decided option (a) the same day: resolve the login-shell environment at start-up); **secure-config fix-ups at every command start** → **Story 9.37**.
- **Pre-existing op-log divergences** found by the 9.26 follow-up review → **Story 9.38** (`edit` of the current `@`), **Story 9.39** (v0.44.0 undo stack), **Story 9.40** (`finish_verb`'s missing `has_changes()` gate: describe/fetch/undo no-change ops).
- **Verified while writing 9.37:** the CLI's `default_config_migrations()` (key renames via `jj_lib::config::migrate`, applied at every start-up) is EMPTY at v0.44.0 and Zanshin applies none — no gap today; re-check at every `jj-lib` pin bump.
- **`--when.commands` scopes per verb** → **Story 9.41** (`9-41-when-commands-config-scopes-per-verb`), added after the 9.27 second follow-up review surfaced it; Ralph asked for a story. Every Zanshin verb resolves the config with its CLI counterpart's command name (one mapping table), and the per-verb refresh reloads the workspace only when the resolved settings differ.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 — Story 9.27 second follow-up review

_Second follow-up review of `spec-9-27-effective-user-settings-config-stack.md` (commit 84a4df8 only, verified against HEAD 7b7b506). Patched in the same pass: the creation verbs now run the CLI's start-up config check first (a user-level fault wins over every path/precondition error and creates nothing, as `jj git init` / `jj git clone` @ v0.44.0), plus scoped-identity witnesses for clone, import, symlinked paths and `--when.workspaces`. One item is deferred:_

- **`--when.commands` scopes never apply outside the config verbs** — **MOVED 2026-09-24 → Story 9.41** (`9-41-when-commands-config-scopes-per-verb`; Ralph asked for a story — it maps every verb to its CLI command name). [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/config.rs::load_with` / `resolve_raw`] — the CLI sets its command name before the start-up resolution (`cli/src/cli_util.rs:4581` @ v0.44.0) and `settings_for_new_workspace` clones that `config_env` (`:377`), so a `[[--scope]]` with `--when.commands = ["git clone"]` applies to what `jj git clone` writes (and `["describe"]` to what `jj describe` writes). Zanshin resolves with `command: None` for every verb except Story 9.28's config verbs (documented in the `config.rs` module doc), so such a scope never applies — creation, open or mutation. Pre-existing; the fix is a CLI command name per Zanshin verb (e.g. `Repo::clone` → `"git clone"`, `convert_import` → `"git init"`) threaded into `resolve_raw` for every verb, not a local change. Severity low (rare config); needs Ralph's call on whether to map every verb.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 while implementing Story 9.32 (warning channel)

_The deferrals of `spec-9-32-warning-channel-every-envelope.md` (§ Deferrals, plus two from its review pass); every other CLI @ v0.44.0 `ui.warning_*` site on a ported path is a registered warning code (`docs/rust-error-codes.md` § Warning Codes, with the out-of-scope list and its owners)._

- **`trunk()` resolution fallback is not ported** [CLI `cli/src/cli_util.rs:545-552` @ v0.44.0] — the CLI falls back to `root()` (with a warning) when the configured `trunk()` alias fails to resolve; Zanshin has no such fallback, so neither the behaviour nor its warnings exist. Behaviour gap, not a warning gap: port the fallback and its warnings come with it. Severity low.
- **`git fetch`'s failed-ref NAMES have no typed payload** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/remotes.rs::git_fetch`] — fetch reports its import's unreadable refs as the `jj.git_import_failed` warning only (the names live in the message); unlike `git import`, `MutationResult` has no `failed_ref_names`. Story 9.30 reworks fetch and owns the payload. Severity low.
- **"Workspace no longer exists" is an error in Zanshin, a warning in the CLI** [CLI `cli/src/cli_util.rs:2437-2442` @ v0.44.0; Zanshin `mutate.rs::finish_transaction`] — when an operation (e.g. an `undo`) leaves the current workspace without a working-copy commit, the CLI warns "The current workspace '…' no longer exists after this operation. The working copy was left untouched."; Zanshin errors after committing instead. Recorded divergence, not a warning code. Severity low.
- **`status`'s conflict lines have no typed data** [CLI `cli/src/commands/status.rs` @ v0.44.0] — the CLI prints unresolved-conflict and conflicted-bookmark lines through its status formatter (not `ui.warning_*`, so no warning code); `Status`/`Bookmarks` carry no conflict facts for them. Severity low; a payload widening when the UI needs it.
- **Three CLI start-up environment steps are not ported for every verb** (review pass) [CLI `cli/src/cli_util.rs:934-966`, `:1092-1114` @ v0.44.0; Zanshin `snapshot.rs::CommandEnv::load` (was `Knobs::read` until Story 9.34), `repo.rs::Repo::resolve_workspace_root`] — `WorkspaceCommandEnvironment::new` loads the fileset, revset AND template alias tables and parses `revsets.short-prefixes` (fallback `revsets.log`) for EVERY workspace command, `jj workspace root` included (`workspace_helper_no_snapshot`). Zanshin's pre-verb snapshot ports the fileset/revset alias loads and the `immutable_heads()` parse only: the `[template-aliases]` load (its declaration parser is jj-cli's `TemplateAliasParser`), the short-prefixes parse on non-graph verbs and `zanshin_workspace_root`'s environment (it loads no config) are missing — and with them their warnings AND their start-up refusals (an unparseable `revsets.short-prefixes` fails every CLI command). Recorded in the registry's out-of-scope list. Severity low.
- ~~**Double fault: alias warnings missing when a snapshot knob is wrong-typed**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-24 (Story 9.34): the alias tables and the `immutable_heads()` parse load first (`snapshot::CommandEnv`, before the lock); the knobs are read inside the snapshot step, so the alias warnings precede a knob refusal, as in the CLI. (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/snapshot.rs::Knobs::read`] — the knobs (`snapshot.auto-track`, `snapshot.max-new-file-size`, the ignore files) are read BEFORE the revset-alias load and the `immutable_heads()` parse; the CLI loads the alias tables at start-up and reads the knobs inside `snapshot_working_copy`, so its error output carries the alias warnings Zanshin's error envelope lacks. Pre-existing knob-read order — Story 9.34 moves the knob reads to the CLI's place, which fixes this too. Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 while implementing Story 9.33 (creation paths report)

_The deferrals of `spec-9-33-creation-paths-report-identity-snapshot.md` (§ Deferrals). Every other CLI @ v0.44.0 `ui.warning_*` site on the creation flows is a registered warning code or an out-of-scope entry in `docs/rust-error-codes.md` § Warning Codes._

- ~~**`Repo::clone` into an EXISTING jj workspace deletes its `.jj/` — data loss**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-24 (`spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md`): the rollback now runs only for a `.jj/` the clone created; the refusal itself is unchanged (full non-empty-destination parity stays Story 9.20's). Same bug as the Story 3.4a entry above.
- **The CLI's start-up environment on `clone` / `init`** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::Repo::init` / `Repo::clone`; CLI `cli/src/cli_util.rs:936-966` @ v0.44.0, `for_workable_repo`, run twice by `jj git clone`] — the alias-table loads, the `immutable_heads()` / short-prefixes / template parses, their warnings and their start-up refusals, and `clone`'s became-immutable check in its checkout finish (which needs that parsed set) are not ported to the non-converting creations. The conversions DO build the start-up environment (their alias and `immutable_heads()` warnings are ported; Story 9.34). Comes with the start-up-environment port Story 9.32 deferred; for `clone` it is Story 9.20's parity. Severity low.
- **`clone` warns `jj.identity_missing` once, the CLI up to three times** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::clone_into`] — the CLI finishes `add git remote`, `fetch from git remote into empty repo` and `check out git remote's branch` separately (`clone.rs:352`, `:457`, `:314`) and warns after each; Zanshin writes one `clone` operation and warns once. Converges when Story 9.20 splits the operation. Severity low.
- **No Dart real-cdylib witness for a SUCCESSFUL clone** [`packages/zanshin_core/test/worker/creation_test.dart`] — the clone's fetch runs a `git` subprocess whose `Child::wait()` fails inside any `dart test` process (the Dart VM reaps its children — the same limitation `mutate_test.dart` records for `gitFetch`). The clone's `jj.checkout_files_skipped` witness is `crates/zanshin-ffi/tests/repo_ffi.rs::clone_into_a_colliding_target_warns_that_a_checkout_was_skipped` (plain `cargo test`); the Dart test covers `cloneRepo`'s refusal arm. Also means a clone from a Flutter app process may hit the same wait failure — unverified. **Check (Story 6.4a, when it wires the app's fetch):** run a `cloneRepo` and a `gitFetch` from the running macOS app through the worker against a `file://` source; if either fails with "Failed to wait for the git process", the app needs the same fix the test harness lacks. Severity low.
- ~~**The conversion reads its snapshot knobs before the snapshot starts**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-24 (Story 9.34): `external_arm` builds the start-up environment right after `init_git_refs` and before `maybe_add_gitignore` (`for_workable_repo`, `git/init.rs:252`); the knobs are read inside `maybe_snapshot`'s snapshot step, so a wrong-typed knob fails with a PARTIAL `precondition` (`git_head_imported`, `was_snapshot_taken`) — pinned by `tests/creation_warnings.rs` (`a_wrong_typed_snapshot_knob_fails_inside_the_conversion_snapshot`, `a_bad_immutable_heads_alias_fails_before_the_conversion_snapshot`). (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs::external_arm`] — `Knobs::read` runs before `maybe_snapshot`, so a wrong-typed `snapshot.*` knob fails the conversion with NO precondition, while the CLI reads the knobs inside `snapshot_working_copy`, after `maybe_snapshot`'s lock and colocated `import git head` (`cli_util.rs:1331-1416` @ v0.44.0). The verb precondition has the same order (Story 9.34); **extend 9.34's scope to the conversion.** Severity low.
- **`Repo::init` over a directory that already holds `.git/`** (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::Repo::init`; CLI `cli/src/commands/git/init.rs:198-234` @ v0.44.0] — Zanshin creates an internal-git workspace beside the `.git/`; the CLI runs the External flow (default `git.colocate = true`: refs import, snapshot, warnings) or refuses ("Did not create a jj repo because there is an existing Git repo in this directory."). Pre-existing; Story 9.20's init parity. Severity low.
- **A clone that fails after the CLI's `add git remote` finish lacks that finish's identity warning** (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::clone_into`] — the CLI finishes `add git remote origin` (`clone.rs:352`) before fetching, so an empty identity warns even when the fetch fails; Zanshin commits no remote operation. Part of the op-split divergence above (Story 9.20). Severity low.
- **No end-to-end witness of `jj.clone_cleanup_failed` through `Repo::clone`** (review pass) [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::roll_back_clone`] — pinned at the helper and on a synthetic FFI envelope only. The one fault-free trigger was the data-loss path above, which `spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md` closed (2026-09-24); an end-to-end witness now needs a fault seam (a `.jj/` the clone created that cannot be removed). Severity low.
- **The `zanshin_core` barrel exports neither `RepoHandle`, `CreatedRepoHandle` nor `Precondition`** (review pass) [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/zanshin_core.dart`] — the app cannot NAME the creation result's types without a `src/` import (inference still exposes `.warnings` / `.precondition`). `RepoHandle` and `Precondition` were already absent. Severity low; export them when the UI first needs the names. **Picked up 2026-09-26 by Epic 10** (10.1 / 10.5 / 10.8 / 10.9 each export the types they consume).
- ~~**A failed creation's warnings are visible nowhere on the Dart side until Story 9.35**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.35): `_createdOrThrow` / `_handleOrThrow` → `_envelopeToException` go through the shared `decodeEnvelopeErrorInternal`, so a refused `initRepo` / `cloneRepo` / `convert` throws a `ZanshinFfiException` carrying the creation's `warnings` (e.g. `jj.clone_cleanup_failed`) and, for a conversion, its PARTIAL `precondition`; witnessed by `test/worker/error_reports_test.dart::a_failed_conversion_keeps_its_warnings_and_partial_snapshot_report`. (follow-up review) [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart::_createdOrThrow`] — before Story 9.33 the conversion's warnings were at least traced (`log_dropped("convert")`); now they ride the error envelope, which Dart turns into a `ZanshinFfiException` of code and context only, so a failed `cloneRepo`'s `jj.clone_cleanup_failed` or a failed `convert`'s refs-step warnings leave no trace in the app (MCP / Rust callers do get them). Story 9.35's scope (creation verbs' error results) closes it. Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 while fixing the clone-rollback data loss (spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md)

- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md`
  summary: A clone into an existing jj workspace is refused only as a generic `CloneFailed` carrying jj-lib's "The destination repo (…) already exists" text — no typed code, unlike convert's `RepoError::AlreadyJjRepo`.
  evidence: A UI that wants to offer "open it instead" would have to string-match the reason. The CLI refuses any non-empty destination first ("Destination path exists and is not an empty directory", `cli/src/commands/git/clone.rs:187-192` @ v0.44.0) — Story 9.20's destination parity decides the typed refusal (and its code) together with that check. Severity low.
- source_spec: `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/spec-clone-rollback-keeps-existing-jj.md`
  summary: The conversions' rollback removes a DANGLING `.jj` symlink it did not create.
  evidence: `convert_colocated` / `convert_import` gate on `detect_repo`, whose `.jj` check follows symlinks, so a dangling `.jj` link passes as "no jj repo"; `init_external_git` then refuses it (`DestinationExists`) and the rollback `remove_dir_all(<root>/.jj)` deletes the link (`crates/zanshin-jj/src/convert.rs::convert_colocated_steps`, `convert_import_steps`). Pre-existing; only a broken symlink is lost (no workspace — a real `.jj/` is refused by `detect_repo` first). Fix: the clone's `NotFound`-only pre-existence guard. Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-24 while reviewing Story 9.34 (snapshot knobs after the Git head import)

_The deferrals of `spec-9-34-snapshot-knobs-after-git-head-import.md` (frontmatter `deferred:`), mirrored here. All pre-existing since Story 9.26 / 9.22 unless noted._

- **A snapshot-knob fault's `jj.config_error` reason carries a `config error: ` prefix, a start-up-environment fault's does not** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/wc_ops.rs::auto_tracking_matcher` / `snapshot_options`, `mutate.rs::cfg`] — the two wc_ops helpers map `ConfigError` with `cfg`, which keeps its Display ("config error: …"), and `PreconditionError::Config`'s Display adds it again ("config error: config error: …"). Pre-existing for `snapshot.auto-track` and file track/untrack's own reads; Story 9.34 moved the precondition's `snapshot.max-new-file-size` read onto the helper (bare before) and the non-table `[fileset-aliases]` fault the other way (bare now, via `CommandEnv::load`). No test pins the exact context text. **Fix:** one form (bare, as `From<ConfigError>` gives) in both helpers + `assert_eq` on the reason. Severity low.
- **The error registry's `jj.config_error` row does not list the pre-verb snapshot** [`docs/rust-error-codes.md:56`] — neither the start-up refusal (before the Git import/export lock, nothing written) nor the snapshot-knob refusal (after a colocated `import git head`, which stays; partial precondition). The FFI wrappers' rustdoc carries both. Severity low.
- **`zanshin_file_track` / `_untrack`'s `jj.config_error` bullets name only their own keys** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/file_ops.rs`] — the pre-verb snapshot also refuses an unparseable `snapshot.auto-track` and a bad `immutable_heads()` alias / non-table alias layer on both. Severity low.
- **`jj.stale_working_copy` "nothing is written" is untrue after a colocated head import** [every FFI wrapper's stale bullet, `docs/rust-error-codes.md:79`] — `snapshot_impl` commits `import git head` before `snapshot_working_copy`'s stale check refuses (as the CLI, `cli_util.rs:1298-1326`, `:2079` @ v0.44.0). Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-25 while implementing Story 9.35 (Dart error results keep precondition + warnings)

_Implementing `spec-9-35-dart-error-results-keep-precondition-warnings.md` (bridge-only: no Rust, schema or codegen change) surfaced one Rust-side gap outside its scope:_

- **A verb that panics after its precondition loses the report** [`crates/zanshin-ffi/src/envelope.rs::catch`, panic arm] — `catch` maps `Err(panic)` to `err_never_unwind(INTERNAL_PANIC, …, None)`: the `internal.panic` envelope carries no `precondition` and no `warnings`, even when the pre-verb snapshot already ran (and maybe wrote operations) before the panic. Dart now surfaces every error envelope's report on `ZanshinFfiException`, so this is the one RUST error arm that still arrives empty. The worker's own synthesized envelopes carry none either (`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart`): `ffi.invalid_handle` from `_invalidHandleEnvelope` (no verb ran, so there is nothing to lose) and `internal.dispatch_error` from the dispatch `catch` (a Dart-side dispatch failure, which may follow Rust work whose report is then lost). Only reachable through a panic (the `catch` is a backstop the code aims never to hit). **Fix (Rust):** keep the partial `CallReport` reachable from the panic arm (e.g. record it outside the unwind boundary before the verb body runs). Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-25 while reviewing Story 9.35

_The review pass of `spec-9-35-dart-error-results-keep-precondition-warnings.md` (frontmatter `deferred`) deferred one pre-existing item:_

- **The `zanshin_core` barrel does not export `ZanshinFfiException` either** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/zanshin_core.dart`] — extends the Story 9.33 barrel item above (`RepoHandle`, `CreatedRepoHandle`, `Precondition`): since Story 9.35 the thrown `ZanshinFfiException` carries every failed command's `precondition` and `warnings`, but the app can only name the exception (and the report's `Precondition` type) through a `src/` import. Nothing breaks today (no reference in `app/`). Export them when the UI first reads the error report. Severity low. **Picked up 2026-09-26 by Epic 10** (10.5 exports `ZanshinFfiException` / `Precondition`).

## Surfaced 2026-09-25 while implementing Story 9.36 (Finder launch sees the login-shell environment)

_Implementing `spec-9-36-finder-launch-login-shell-environment.md` left these gaps by design (the spec's Never list) or outside its scope:_

- **jj-lib's other subprocesses and gix's in-process reads still see the launchd environment** [`jj-lib-0.44.0/src/gpg_signing.rs:187,269`, `ssh_signing.rs:157`, `git_backend.rs:925` (`run_git_gc`); gix's config reads — e.g. `wc_ops.rs::base_ignores`' `config_snapshot()` / `gix::config::File::from_globals()` — read `HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `GIT_CONFIG_*` from the process] — jj-lib offers no environment hook for signing or `git gc`, and gix reads the process environment directly. A Finder-launched Zanshin can therefore sign with a different `gpg`/`ssh-keygen` (or none on `PATH`) and resolve a different global git config than the terminal `jj`. Fix needs an upstream hook (or `std::env::set_var` at start-up, which the spec rules out). Severity medium for signing users.
- **The UI does not surface `env.login_shell_unresolved` yet** [`app/lib/main.dart::_adoptShellEnvironment` (was `_startApp` before the 2026-09-25 redirect)] — the warning is only logged (`zanshin.bootstrap`, `<code>: <message>`). A user whose rc file hangs or fails silently gets the launchd environment with no visible hint. UI is Ralph's scope.
- **Variables the rc file UNSETS stay in the `git` subprocess environment** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/environment.rs::git_subprocess_options`] — jj-lib applies `GitSubprocessOptions::environment` with `Command::envs` on top of the inherited process environment (no `env_clear`), so the map can add and override but never remove. The config stack is unaffected (it reads the adopted map alone). Severity low.
- ~~**A timed-out interactive shell started from a terminal is still killed**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.36 redirect):** the login shell is now read in Dart (`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/environment/login_shell.dart`) with `Process.start(…, mode: ProcessStartMode.detachedWithStdio)` — the Dart runtime `setsid()`s the child, so the shell has no controlling terminal to take over, and a timeout kills a process that holds none. zanshin-jj spawns nothing any more. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/environment.rs::read_login_environment`, timeout arm] — a real `zsh -i` started under a controlling terminal (e.g. Zanshin run via `flutter run`) makes itself the terminal's foreground process group; on success Zanshin now lets it exit on its own, but on TIMEOUT it is SIGKILLed and can leave the terminal's foreground group dead (the launching tool stopped by SIGTTIN). The fix needs `setsid` for the child, which is unstable in std and `unsafe` is not allowed in `zanshin-jj`. Severity low.
- **`zanshin-mcp` never adopts** [`crates/zanshin-mcp`] — the MCP server is launched by its client with the client's environment and keeps reading the process environment; no adoption call exists there (spec: no `zanshin-mcp` change). Revisit if an MCP client launched from the Dock shows the same drift.

## Surfaced 2026-09-25 while implementing Story 9.37 (secure-config fix-ups at every command start)

_Implementing `spec-9-37-secure-config-fixups-every-command.md` left these residual divergences from the CLI (the spec's Never list or outside its scope):_

- **The `&self` accessors run no fix-up** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/repo.rs::log_defaults` / `workspace_root` / `resolve_workspace_root`] — the spec keeps them side-effect-free, so they read the state as of the handle's last open or precondition-running verb. The CLI's `jj workspace root` is a command and runs the start-up fix-up (a copied repo forked, a deleted secure dir regenerated, a legacy file migrated) before it answers. Severity low.
- **A malformed id file keeps Zanshin's path-named refusal** [`config.rs::SecureLayer::fix_up` → `resolved_path()` pre-check] — `<dir>/config-id: invalid config ID (expected 20 ASCII hex characters)` (`jj.config_error`), where the CLI fails with its internal "Failed to determine the secure config for a repo: Found an invalid config ID" (`command_error.rs:754-758`). Same refusal, nothing written either way; only the text differs. Severity low.
