- **The `edit` halves fix up under a broken stack** [`config_cmd.rs::file_read_with` / `file_write_with` → `config::fix_up_layers`] — they run the fix-up of both layers even when a user/system/repo file does not parse, whereas every CLI command (its `jj config edit` included) refuses at start-up before the fix-ups (a broken system/user file) or between them (a broken repo file stops the workspace fix-up). Deliberate: the halves are the GUI's repair path and load no stack. Severity low.
- **`JJ_RANDOMNESS_SEED` seeds separate RNGs** [`config.rs::secure_rng`, used by `SecureLayer::fix_up` (one per pass) and `SecureLayer::create_path` (one per call)] — the CLI shares ONE seeded `ConfigEnv.rng` per command, so a fix-up followed by a `config path --workspace` draws different ids than the CLI would. Worse, a seeded COPY whose fork draws its original's id writes into the original's secure dir, and the kind is then classified `ConfigRegenerated` (same id before and after) instead of `ConfigCopied`. Only with the seed set (tests, reproducibility runs). Severity low.
- **The creation paths' final re-open runs the fix-up** [`repo.rs::init_workspace` / `clone_reported`, `convert.rs` → `Repo::open`] — a no-op on the freshly created workspace (no id file, no legacy file); its warnings, if any, are dropped via `warning::log_dropped("Repo::open", …)`. Severity none today.
- **Every test-binary run leaves its XDG temp dir behind** [`crates/test-helpers/src/lib.rs::leaked_xdg_config_home`, `TempDir::keep`] — one kept dir under `$TMPDIR` per test binary per run (the pinned `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` must outlive every test thread), plus whatever the fix-ups migrated into it. The OS temp cleaner reclaims them eventually. Severity low.
- **The UI does not show the fix-up notices yet** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/handle.dart::OpenedRepoHandle.warnings`] — `openRepo`'s warnings (and the config results' / verb results' `jj.config_*` notices) have no consumer in `app/`; a user whose legacy config was migrated is not told. UI is Ralph's scope.
- ~~**No test pins that the bootstrap adopts BEFORE the app exists**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-09-25 (Story 9.36 redirect):** the adoption moved into `ZanshinBootSequence` as the injected `adoptShellEnvironment` step (started alongside `openSettings`, awaited before `startApp`); `zanshin_bootstrap_test.dart` pins that `startApp` is not called before it completes, and that its failure fails the boot. Original entry: [`app/lib/main.dart::_startApp`; found by the 9.36 review] — `_startApp` is the private composition root and `zanshin_bootstrap_test.dart` injects its own `startApp`, so dropping the `adoptLoginShellEnvironment()` call, or moving it after `zanshinAppFactory.create()`, would ship with every witness green. Needs a seam (e.g. the adopt-and-log step as a small bootstrap helper the app tests drive). UI/bootstrap wiring is Ralph's call. Severity low.

## Surfaced 2026-09-26 while researching the commit-graph view and authoring Stories 4.10–4.12 / Epic 10 (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph showed a mockup of the main view (commit cards at (row, lane), elbow lines, two-axis scrolling; `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/research/commit-graph-mockup-2026-09-26.webp`) and asked how to build it without a `ListView`, how to virtualize it and how to size the scrollbars before everything is loaded. Research with verified evidence and a tested POC: `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/research/technical-commit-graph-virtualization-research-2026-09-26.md` (POC: `app/lib/main4.dart` + `app/lib/src/features/graph_poc/`). That became Stories 4.10–4.12 (graph backend/bridge; Epic 4 reopened) and Epic 10 (the view itself, built by Claude at Ralph's explicit request). The items below fall outside those stories:_

- **The CLI draws at most one missing-edge stub per node; Zanshin emits every `Missing` edge.** Low.
  - Evidence: `cli/src/commands/log.rs:250-272` @ v0.44.0 keeps only the LAST `Missing` target and appends it after the direct/indirect edges. `crates/zanshin-graph/src/stream.rs:56-60` and `crates/zanshin-graph/src/lane.rs:266-279` keep each `Missing` edge in stream order.
  - Lanes are unaffected: a `Missing` edge never reserves a lane and never continues the own lane.
  - But a renderer that draws one stub per `Missing` edge shows more stubs than `jj log` for a node with several out-of-set parents.
  - Found while verifying the graph research at the pinned tag. Not in the 4.8 parity catalog (`_bmad-output/specs/spec-4-8-log-rows-cli-parity/cli-log-parity.md:71`).
  - **Follow-up:** collapse the edges in `stream.rs` (parity at the source) or draw one stub per row in the view (Epic 10). Decide together with Story 4.12's choice on whether a `Missing` edge's target id stays on the wire.
- **No lane cap — `all()` over many long-lived heads can be hundreds of lanes wide.** Low until measured.
  - Evidence: `crates/zanshin-graph/src/lane.rs:211-220` (slots are cleared, never removed), `:249-251` (each child's continuation keeps its own lane for a parent that has not arrived yet), `:389-396`.
  - Width is the peak number of concurrently open segments: every branch emitted above its fork point holds a lane down to the fork row.
  - The committed fixtures peak at lane 3 (`crates/zanshin-graph/tests/stream.rs:388-392`), so this is unmeasured on a real repo. Story 4.11's `lane_count` makes it visible.
  - Whether the view needs horizontal compaction or a cap is a product decision for Epic 10.
- **The graph perf numbers come from a loose-object fixture.** Low.
  - Evidence: `large-10k` has 30,003 loose git objects and no pack (research 2026-09-26). A cold per-commit load costs 25-37 µs in batches of up to 200 ids, but 115-190 µs in longer runs; the cause was not investigated.
  - Story 4.10's bench runs on the same fixture, so its content-page numbers may not transfer to a packed repo.
  - **Follow-up:** one run on a `git gc`-packed copy before tuning the page size.
- **The default `revsets.log` selects every commit on the committed and generated fixtures.** Low.
  - Evidence: `crates/zanshin-jj/src/revset.rs:33` `DEFAULT_LOG_REVSET`. `cargo xtask gen-fixture` sets local bookmarks only — no tags, no remote bookmarks (`crates/xtask/src/tasks/gen_fixture.rs:263-268`, `:327-332`) — and tiny ships no tags (`crates/zanshin-jj/tests/view.rs:88`). So `trunk()` is `root()` and `builtin_log()` is effectively `all()`.
  - The research's "the default log grows with mutable work, not with history" is inferred from the definition (`cli/src/config/revsets.toml` @ v0.44.0), not measured.
  - Every default-revset number taken on these fixtures is an `all()` number.
- **`--limit` / `--reversed` stay unported, and their revisit trigger disappears if windowing is replaced.** Low; decide when the view offers "oldest first".
  - Evidence: `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md` Story 4.4 "Delivered" note; `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/epic-4-context.md:28` says to revisit "when windowing returns".
  - Story 4.10 proposes replacing row-range windowing with complete geometry. If Ralph accepts, windowing never returns.
  - `--reversed` is not presentational: the CLI collects the (limited) topo stream and renders `reverse_graph` of it (`cli/src/commands/log.rs:235-239` @ v0.44.0). Those lanes differ from a flipped forward layout, so an "oldest first" view needs its own geometry pass.
- **Fallback if whole-revset geometry ever gets too big: let the scroll extent grow while rows stream in.** Low; not needed at the 10k baseline.
  - Evidence: rows are final when emitted (`crates/zanshin-graph/src/lane.rs:149-154`; research §3).
  - A view could therefore append rows and grow its content height monotonically as the stream delivers them, with the scrollbar thumb shrinking.
  - Caveat: until the stream ends, dragging to the end means nothing, and "scroll to @" works only once `@`'s row has arrived.
  - Record it as the escape hatch, not a plan.
- **`abandon` does not report the bookmarks it deleted** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/mutate.dart:167-211`; CLI `cli/src/commands/abandon.rs:138-163` @ v0.44.0]
  - `MutationResult` carries `newOpId`, `newChangeIds`, `abandonedChangeIds`, `workingCopyChangeId`, `finish` and `warnings`, and nothing else.
  - The CLI diffs the local bookmarks before and after and prints "Deleted bookmarks: …". When a deleted bookmark had tracked remotes, it also prints a push hint.
  - 10.9 cannot show this fact and must not work it out in Dart. Backend follow-up: a field on the abandon result. Severity low.
- **The shipped verbs lack CLI parameters that the UI would have to offer** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/handle.dart:228` `describe`, `:242` `newChange`, `:271` `abandon`, `:145` `graphSlice`; CLI @ v0.44.0 `abandon.rs:61`, `:65`; `new.rs:72`, `:76`, `:118`, `:158`; `describe.rs:53-57`; `log.rs:93`, `:143`, `:236`]
  - `abandon` has no `--retain-bookmarks` and no `--restore-descendants`.
  - `new` has no `-m`, `--no-edit`, `-A` or `-B`.
  - `describe` takes one change id where the CLI takes revsets.
  - `graphSlice` has no `--limit`, no `--reversed` and no path arguments.

  Under the pass-through rule each of these is a backend gap. Epic 10 offers only what the verbs expose (10.9). Severity medium (parity).
- **No backend path from a file edit to a graph refresh** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/events.dart:17-33`; jj-lib 0.44.0 `lib/src/config/misc.toml:10`, `lib/src/fsmonitor.rs:290`; `handle.dart:961` `snapshot()`]
  - The watcher reports only op-heads and checkout changes. A file edit writes no operation until something snapshots, so @'s `empty` badge and its content go stale while the user edits.
  - jj's own answer is `fsmonitor.watchman.register-snapshot-trigger`. It is off by default and runs the `jj` binary. FR-RX-2 (Story 3.10's external-edit test) assumes that trigger.
  - 10.5 asks Ralph for a UI refresh policy. A Zanshin-side snapshot trigger, such as a debounced `snapshot()` on file events, would be its own backend story. Severity medium.
- **The UI has no open-project flow** [`app/lib/src/features/projects/home_dialog.dart` lists the mock `kProjects` (`app/lib/src/shared/data/mock_data.dart:377`)] — the graph needs an open `RepoHandle`, and the FR-JJ-17 detect → open/convert UI has no story. Until one exists, 10.5 opens an explicitly given path. Severity medium: it blocks real use, not development.
- **The running app does not show the real UI** [`app/lib/src/features/app/zanshin_app.dart:26-28`, `:124-149`; `app/lib/src/shared/theme/zanshin_colors.dart:170`]
  - `ZanshinApp` renders the Yaru test page, and `HomePage` → `AppShell` (mock data) is "temporarily unused".
  - `context.zc` force-unwraps a `ZanshinColors` extension that the current themes do not register.
  - Where the graph mounts, and under which theme, is Ralph's call. 10.1 registers `GraphTheme` only in its demo host. Severity low.
- **The mockup's window chrome has no story** [mockup; the existing mock widgets in `app/lib/src/features/shell/main_nav_rail.dart`, `status_bar.dart` and `window_title_bar.dart`]
  - The top toolbar (Fetch / Pull / Push / New).
  - The status bar ("@ feature/new-ui", "repo clean", "142 ops", versions, "⌘K palette").
  - The nav rail (history / branches / graph / comments / undo / settings).

  "Pull" has no jj verb: `jj git fetch` plus a rebase is not one command, so it needs a decision under the CLI-parity rule. Severity low.
- **The graph view supersedes the mock UI** [`app/lib/src/features/commits/commit_card.dart` (`BranchPanel`, a `ListView`), `app/lib/src/features/history/history_column.dart` (a `ListView` with lane dots), `app/lib/src/features/branches/branches_panel.dart`, `app/lib/src/shared/data/mock_data.dart`, and `AppShell`'s branch columns (`app/lib/src/features/shell/app_shell.dart:144-164`)] — retiring these is Ralph's call. 10.8 builds a real-data panel modelled on the mock `commit_detail.dart` / `diff_block.dart`, which stay. Severity none.
- **Ralph's own note (2026-09-26): clean up the demo entry points** [`app/lib/main2.dart`, `main3.dart`, `main4.dart` (the graph POC)] — 10.1 needs one fake-data demo host for visual review, and which file hosts it is part of that cleanup. Severity none.
- **Story 9.25's backend item 1 is stale** [epics.md Story 9.25 item 1; `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart:1537-1543`] — it says "No generic repo-config writer exists yet", but Story 9.28 shipped `configSet(ConfigScope.repo, …)`, which 10.7's Save uses. Re-scope 9.25 when it is refined. Severity low.
- **No capability query without a drop target** [epics.md Story 9.31, Non-goals] — the target-less "what can I do with S" query is marked "own story if wanted". Without it, 10.6's context menu cannot grey out verbs with the CLI's reason. Severity low. **Update 2026-10-14:** severity raised to medium. 10.15's card menu now exists, and its recommended tooltips (a disabled row shows the backend's refusal reason) need this query; 9.31's dated note proposes it as a variant.
- **No identity-remedy UI** — a `jj.identity_missing` warning ("set your identity", 9.32's motivating example) has no story. 10.9 only surfaces warnings. Severity low.
- **No golden-test setup** — the repo has no golden tests, and Inter / JetBrainsMono would need to load in tests. Epic 10's visual review is therefore manual, in the demo host. Add a golden setup only if Ralph wants one. Severity none.

- **The bookmark list cannot show the design's rows before Story 9.29** [`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/ffi/bookmarks.dart:26-67`; `handle.dart:183`; design GD:21, GV:242, GV:481-497]. Medium (the designed panel's main use case).

  - The design's total counts every commit, independent of the revset.
  - Story 4.10 as written returns `total_rows` for the requested revset only.
  - An index-only `|all()|` count must be added to 4.10's geometry result, or a separate count call. Otherwise 10.7 shows the shown count only.
- **Every time-based mode needs every commit's timestamp before layout** [jj-lib 0.44.0 `default_index/readonly.rs:693-696`: generation numbers, no timestamps; design GV:161-172; spike SPEC §2, §4.2]. Medium; it decides the cost of Time and free at 10k–100k.
  - Time rows depend on each commit's bucket, and the spike's `free` y depends on each commit's time. The index carries no timestamps.
  - Story 4.10 as written moves timestamps into lazily loaded content.
  - One of three options must be chosen in 4.10/4.11:
    - the geometry pass loads every commit, about 1.2 s at 10k, giving back most of 4.10's gain;
    - a persistent commit-id → timestamp cache (commit-intrinsic, so it never goes stale);
    - the time-based modes are restricted to small revsets.
  - Story 10.13's outcome does not remove this; only a Compact-only product would.
- **What Epic 10 needs from the re-plans of 4.11 and 4.10** [GV:140-225; `spikes/graph-layout-ab/SPEC.md`]. Medium.
  - **4.11, modes.** All three modes behind a mode enum (compact, time, free), with rows in 1/U units plus `unit` and `rows` (SPEC §3). 10.13 decides which ones the product exposes, but 4.11's build does not wait for it.
  - **4.11, still open** (the spike does not fix these; each needs Ralph or the re-plan):
    - blocked-merge routing, which runs behind cards and collinear with the chain and pushes the continuation off its lane (prototype defect 2; SPEC D9, §11.4). The spike's synthetic inputs have many, e.g. 229 blocked merges on `synth-10k` (compact, greedy), so Epic 10's route invariant stays a counted report until this is fixed;
    - free-mode buses passing behind cards (SPEC D8, §11.1), if free mode ships;
    - a real source for the hand-set `priority` (SPEC §11.2; a stand-in today);
    - lane-order stability across reloads (SPEC §11.5): "Min. crossings" is global, so one operation can reshuffle columns.
  - **4.10, elided counts.** The layout neither outputs Missing edges nor counts elided commits (SPEC §0, §3, D2). So the stub comes from 4.10's Missing edges, and N, if 10.4 design question (a) keeps it, needs a 4.10 geometry field or a separate count call. The prototype's per-parent DFS is O(h) per Missing parent, and jj's bounded Missing walk differs from it.
  - **Already fixed by the spike**, credited here so the re-plan does not redo them: the Time topological clamp (D1); a loop instead of the `rowOf` recursion (§4.2); no duplicate parent edges (§2, V3); O(n log n) lane packing and O(E log n) blocked checks (§4.3–§4.4); budgeted sifting instead of the factorial / O(E²) optimiser, which also counts crossings of edges that share a node (§4.6, D5).
  - Also: the mock's `wq` default-revset flag is wrong. jj shows 13 of the 22 commits, with the stub under `vr` (GD:1-2, :17), so `graph-data.js` is not a test oracle for the default revset. The spike's design oracle keeps the mock's flags on purpose; 10.1 records a CLI-faithful variant next to it.
- **Design chrome and panels outside Epic 10** [APP:135-171, :206-308, :313-350, :956-972, :1054-1080, :1983-2178, :2263-2271]. Low. They are Ralph's UI, and no Epic 10 story covers them:

  - the nav rail, and the placeholder Workstreams / Reviews / Undo Timeline panels; *(Resolved 2026-10-16 for Workstreams: dropped everywhere, decision 16.26; 11.10 removes the remnants.)*
  - the Projects (Home) dialog, and the Density tweak;
  - dead flyout code that is never opened;
  - App's own Esc handler (APP:2263-2271), which clears the selection of the old column view. That view is no longer mounted (APP:2484).

  The graph's global Esc (GV:541-548) is prototype defect 11, owned by 10.6. This extends the 2026-09-26 bullet "the mockup's window chrome has no story".
- **The planned revset Save lost its only UI consumer** [Story 9.25; Story 9.28's `configSet(ConfigScope.repo, 'revsets.log', …)`; design GV:240-276]. Low.
  - The design's revset control has exactly two presets (`jj log`, `all()`), with no free-text field and no Apply/Save.
  - The first Epic 10 plan's Save to `revsets.log`, and 9.25's presets, Apply and Save, have no UI consumer until Ralph answers 10.7 design question (a).
  - **Follow-up:** re-scope 9.25 accordingly.

## Surfaced 2026-09-26 while re-planning Stories 4.10–4.12 after the layout A/B (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph decided to run the commit-graph layout in Dart on the worker isolate (`spikes/graph-layout-ab`, README "Review 2026-09-26"), and Stories 4.10–4.12 were re-planned on that. Measurements were taken the same day on a copy of a real 1.4k-commit repo kept outside the repo; only counts and timings are recorded. The items below fall outside the re-planned stories, or update earlier bullets of the 2026-09-26 section:_

- **Status of the earlier 2026-09-26 graph bullets after the re-plan.** Low.
  - "The graph perf numbers come from a loose-object fixture": now measured. A commit load costs 3.4–4.8 µs packed against ~122 µs loose, and plain git and the jj CLI show the same split. 4.10 item 8 benches a `git gc`-packed copy next to the committed fixture.
  - "Every time-based mode needs every commit's timestamp": now 4.10 item 4. Loading them per call on request is recommended; a commit-id cache is its open question 1, with a measured trigger.
  - "What Epic 10 needs from the re-plans of 4.11 and 4.10": covered by 4.10 items 1–4 (parent count, Missing edges, `all_count`, anchors, timestamps) and 4.11 items 5–9 (input builder, priority, stability, kept defects, result view). Two gaps remain: its line "no duplicate parent edges (§2, V3)" under "Already fixed by the spike" is wrong (next bullet), and N has no owner (the bullet after).
  - "The PRD's graph FRs still describe the `jj log` lane model": FR-GR-1 / FR-GR-2 / FR-GR-5 get dated notes with the re-plan. Re-pointing FR-GR-4 / NFR-P-2 and `project-context.md:14` is 4.10 item 9.
  - "The CLI draws at most one missing-edge stub per node": moot once 4.12 lands. The topology drops the Missing target id, and the view draws one stub per node (10.4).
  - "No lane cap": about the pvigier lanes, which 4.12 proposes to remove. The design's packing has its own width: 10–12 lanes on the real repo's `all()`, 15–16 on jj's own history, 134–173 on the synthetic stress inputs. Whether the view needs horizontal compaction stays an Epic 10 question.
  - "`--limit` / `--reversed` … revisit trigger disappears": it did, because the windowing amendment was accepted. An "oldest first" view would be a layout option (4.11), not a jj pass-through.
  - `syntheticElided`: the re-plan's Epic 10 fixups stop sending it (10.5 item 2, 10.7 item 9), because the topology call has no synthetic nodes. What `ui.log-synthetic-elided-nodes` means for this view stays 10.4 design question (a).
- **SPEC §2's "jj never yields duplicate targets" is false on real data.** Medium; it decides whether goldens, recordings and the product agree on merges.
  - On the real repo's default revset (12 nodes), a merge carries a Direct and an Indirect edge to the same parent.
  - The stage-2 dumper (`spikes/graph-layout-ab/rust-inputs/src/jj_dump.rs`) keeps the first edge and records the other in its `.meta.json` sidecar (`dropped_duplicate_parents`).
  - 4.11 item 5 keeps V3 as the engine's validation and puts the rule into the input builders: one layout edge per (child, target) at the first position, `direct` if any duplicate is direct (open question).
  - **Follow-up:** SPEC §2 and the dumper adopt the rule before 4.11's goldens and 10.1's recordings are made. `tools/fuzz.mjs` should generate repeated targets for the builder's tests.
- **The elided count N has no owner.** Low; only if 10.4 design question (a) keeps "~ N elided".
  - N depends on the revset (a parent is hidden relative to what the revset shows), so it is neither topology nor commit-intrinsic content, and 10.11's commit-id cache cannot hold it. It needs an index walk per Missing parent.
  - 4.10's Non-goals exclude it, and the re-plan's Epic 10 fixups now say "a follow-up count call".
  - **Follow-up:** if 10.4 (a) keeps N, create a story for an op-pinned count call for the visible nodes, e.g. `elided_counts(operation_id, revset, commit_ids)`.
- **The spike's dumper is the reference input builder and must move onto the product path.** Low.
  - `jj_dump.rs` calls `Repo::walk_graph` and computes the `priority` stand-in itself (`assign_priorities`, `rust-inputs/src/input.rs:147`).
  - 10.1 records its layouts from these dumps until 10.12, and 4.11's end-to-end tests compare the Dart input builder against them.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.11 item 10. The dumper calls `Repo::graph_topology` (+ `commit_contents` for the sidecar) and computes the classes from its anchors exactly as SPEC §11.2 (amended) and 4.11 item 6 do. Re-record 10.1's recordings after the switch.
- **Story 9.25 builds on `graphSlice`.** Low.
  - Evidence: epics.md Story 9.25, Apply evaluates via `graphSlice` and has an "N revisions" line; its open question says Apply "should" reuse `graphSlice` directly.
  - If 4.12 removes the slice path, Apply evaluates through the topology call and uses its node count.
  - **Follow-up:** re-scope 9.25 when it is refined.
- **The worker is one serial isolate for every open repo.** Low until measured.
  - Every handle's commands and the watcher events share one isolate (`packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/isolate.dart:1674-1708`). A layout, a timestamped topology call on a loose store (~1.2 s at 10k) or any other long synchronous command delays everything queued behind it.
  - 4.11 item 9 measures a content page queued behind a layout and names a second `Isolate.spawn` layout isolate as the fallback.
  - **Follow-up:** if the measurement shows stalls that matter, decide between that second isolate and splitting long commands; the same question applies to other slow commands once real repos run in the app.
- **Zanshin never packs the git store, and loose commits load ~30× slower.** Low.
  - Measured 2026-09-26: 3.4–4.8 µs per commit load packed, ~122 µs loose.
  - jj-lib writes new objects loose. Neither jj-lib nor Zanshin runs `git gc` / `jj util gc`, and git's own auto-gc runs only from git commands (e.g. a `git fetch` subprocess).
  - A repo worked only through Zanshin therefore accumulates loose commits. That slows content pages and every timestamped layout (4.10 item 4), and it is one trigger for 4.10's option (c).
  - **Follow-up:** measure on a long-lived, Zanshin-only repo. `jj util gc` parity would be an Epic 9 verb, not a background job.
- **`empty` on merge commits dominates content pages.** Low.
  - `Commit::is_empty` first asks jj's changed-path index (jj-lib 0.44.0 `commit.rs:160-165`). Only when that index does not cover the commit does it merge the parent trees of a merge: 0.78 ms per merge, measured without one.
  - On the real repo that is 115 ms of today's 163 ms `collect_lanes(all())`. The CLI's default `jj log` template pays the same (138 ms).
  - `large-10k` has one merge, so fixture numbers hide this.
  - **Follow-up:** tune 10.11's page and buffer size on a real repo, not on the fixtures, and measure a content page with a changed-path index present (4.10 item 8). Building one is an Epic 9 verb for CLI parity (`jj debug index-changed-paths`), never a background job.
- **Real-repo data stays out of git.** None; a standing rule.
  - The 2026-09-26 real-repo dumps, metadata sidecars (descriptions, authors, bookmarks) and layout outputs live outside the repository.
  - The spike's `.gitignore` covers `/inputs/`, `/outputs/` and `/viewer/data/`.
  - 4.10's and 4.12's benches accept a local repo path and record only node counts and timings.
  - Any new tool that writes real-repo data writes it to a gitignored path or outside the repo.

## Surfaced 2026-09-26 while reviewing the spike viewer: lane order, Free's day axis, jumps and the minimap (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph used the spike viewer (`spikes/graph-layout-ab/viewer/`) on a copy of the real 1.4k-commit repo, kept outside the repo. He asked for four things: a lane order that wastes less space, a day axis in Free, go-to-commit (were jumps tested?) and a minimap for big repos (and how it performs). His current preference is Free, tentatively. The spike built or measured all four the same day. The stories were updated on 2026-09-27: 4.11 items 1, 2 and 11, its Tests and Non-goals, and open questions 8–9; 4.12 item 6; 10.1 item 3; 10.2 items 2 and 9; 10.5 items 2 and 6; 10.6 item 11 and design question (d); 10.13's status note and item 6; and the new Story 10.16. The items below fall outside those updates:_

- **Free's day buckets need a spike change before 4.11's goldens, if 4.11 open question 9 takes them.** Medium; it decides whether goldens, recordings and the product agree on Free's gutter.
  - The viewer computes each node's clamped day itself, from the timestamps in its bundle. Neither spike implementation emits buckets in `free`, so the proposal has no reference output yet.
  - **Follow-up:** once Ralph accepts it, write dated amendments:
    - SPEC §2: `utc_offset_min` is read in free too;
    - SPEC §3: buckets in free, one `day` bucket per local day of T, the root in none, `start` / `end` in 1/U units;
    - SPEC §7.3 I5: today "`[]` outside `time`". A free row can hold two days, so their ranges may share a row.

    Implement them in `dart/` and in the Rust reference so that byte identity still holds, extend `tools/compare.mjs`'s I5 check and `tools/fuzz.mjs`, and re-bundle the viewer from the engine's buckets.
- ~~**`optimized` is still named outside 4.11.**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-09-27 (Ralph dropped `optimized`, 4.11 open question 8): dated notes re-point 10.1 items 10 and 13, 10.5 item 2, 10.12, the Epic 10 header's Lane order tweak and 10.7 design question (f). The Lane order control's label stays open there, because "Compact" collides with the Compact layout. Originally low; it depended on 4.11 open question 8.
  - These places all assume `optimized`: 10.1 (its recordings "each with greedy and optimized lane order", and the anchoring note that "Min. crossings" is global), 10.5 item 2 (the greedy-then-optimized pair) and the design tweak's label "Min. crossings".
  - **Follow-up:** once Ralph picks the default, re-point them at `compact` or drop them, and relabel 10.7's Lane order control.
- **`compact`'s known weaknesses are engine follow-ups, not 4.11 scope.** Low until Ralph adopts `compact`. **Adopted 2026-09-27** as the default (greedy the fallback), so they now concern the default view.
  - A segment is atomic. A long-lived branch that keeps receiving trunk merges moves as a whole. On the real repo it brings back the pattern of Ralph's screenshot for ~40 pitches (spike README "Lane order review 2026-09-26" §6). Candidate: split segments at quiet stretches.
  - At 10k+ commits the budget truncates the search. On synth-10k in compact mode it stops inside the first sweep and ends wider than greedy; on synth-100k only the leftmost dozen columns move. Candidates: a larger or time-based budget, or greedy painted first and `compact` swapped in (4.11 open question 8).
  - Stability under edits (SPEC §11.6): a new commit can re-cut a window. 4.11 item 7's seeded rule covers only `optimized`.
  - **Follow-up:** each one as a dated SPEC amendment with goldens, after the default is decided.
- **Go-to-change needs a backend path for anything but full ids.** — **MOVED 2026-10-14 → Stories 9.25 and 10.21:** 9.25 item 2 (`find_changes`) is that path, and 10.21 resolves the input in the view first. The follow-up's last sentence ("The UI passes the input through and never composes the revset itself") is superseded for structured filters: 10.21's builder writes the revset text (decision (g) of the Epic 10 header's design update 2026-10-14). Original entry: Low; after 10.6 design question (d).
  - The viewer searches what its bundle holds: 12-character change-id and commit-id prefixes and the first description line.
  - In the product, full ids resolve through 4.12's lookups, but descriptions are lazy content (10.11). A description search over the whole revset would load every commit: the same object loads as the timestamps, 6.7 ms on the real repo, 33.6 ms on a packed 10k and ~1.2 s on the loose-object 10k fixture.
  - CLI parity suggests resolving the input on the worker the way the CLI resolves `-r` (id prefixes with jj's refusal of an ambiguous prefix, bookmarks, `description(...)`), then revealing the result.
  - **Follow-up:** a backend story once 10.6 (d) is decided. The UI passes the input through and never composes the revset itself (ADR-0003).
- **With Free as the default, every layout loads every timestamp.** Low; it weighs on 4.10 open question 1. **Free became the default on 2026-09-27 (Ralph)**, so this now applies; 10.13's default is decided, and 4.10's cache question remains.
  - Compact needs no timestamps; Time and Free need all of them before layout (4.10 item 4).
  - The default view would pay that on every operation, and Free's buses behind cards (D8; 11–15 horizontal runs on the real `all()`) would become a default-view issue.
  - **Follow-up:** decide 4.10's cache question together with 10.13's default.
- **Viewer-only details that no story carries.** None; a record.
  - Time's bucket labels and the cards' dates are not localised in the viewer (`?lang=` covers Free's gutter only). 10.2 design question (c) decides for both. (2026-09-27: Time is dropped, and (c) is settled for now: localisable labels, English by default. The app-wide language question is in the next section.)
  - The viewer's merge glyph means "≥ 2 graph edges", a proxy, because its input lacks the parent count. 4.10 item 1's exact `parent_count` fixes that in the product.
  - Mode switches in the viewer keep the card nearest the centre in place; the product re-centres on @ (10.5).
  - The viewer's jump and minimap numbers were taken while another agent's Dart work ran on the same machine (about ±0.5 ms of noise), and its minimap raster ran on the page's main thread. 10.6 item 11 and 10.16 re-measure in Flutter.

## Surfaced 2026-09-27 while recording Ralph's product decisions after the spike viewer (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph decided which layout modes and lane orders the product keeps: Free by default plus Compact, and the `compact` order by default plus greedy; Time, Time+gaps and `optimized` are dropped. He kept three viewer details as intended deviations from the design (crossing gaps always on, content fading under the gutter, the pinned current date) and asked for every edge to leave a card's top at the centre. The stories were updated the same day: the Epic 10 header ("Product decisions 2026-09-27") and the Epic List entries of Epics 4 and 10; 4.10's motivation, item 4 and open question 1; 4.11 (a dated note, its motivation, items 2, 8 and 11, its Tests, open questions 3, 4, 7 and 8); 10.1 items 1–4, 10 and 13; 10.2; 10.3 item 13 and design question (b); 10.4 items 1, 4, 10 and 11, its design status and witnesses; 10.5 items 2 and 3 and a witness; 10.7 item 3, design questions (f) and (h) and a witness; 10.11; 10.12 item 3; 10.13 (item 5 and a dated outcome; closed as done on that outcome, Claude's call, while a Flutter confirmation stays Ralph's); and 10.16 item 6 and design questions (c) and (f). The items below fall outside those stories:_

- **Status of the earlier 2026-09-26 graph bullets after the decisions.** None; a record.
  - "Every time-based mode needs every commit's timestamp before layout": the product is not Compact-only, so it now applies to the default view, Free; 4.10 open question 1 weighs more.
  - "4.11, modes": the product exposes `free` and `compact`; 4.11's dated note proposes porting only these (Ralph to confirm).
  - "free-mode buses passing behind cards …, if free mode ships": free ships as the default, so this is a default-view issue (4.11 open question 4).
  - "lane-order stability across reloads (SPEC §11.5)", with "Min. crossings" global: `optimized` is dropped; `compact`'s stability is SPEC §11.6 and 4.11 open question 8.
- **English-only app, or translated?** Open; app-wide, not Epic 10's. Low; the app's strings are hard-coded English today with no l10n setup (no ARB files), so the answer decides whether one is added before 10.2's two ARB strings.
  - For now the graph's labels stay as the viewer has them: formatted per locale, English by default as in the design (`?lang=de` gives "Heute", "Gestern", "24. Sept.").
  - Ralph is not sure the app should be translated at all: some terms may sound odd in German.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides; then the app's other strings (his UI) and Epic 10's labels follow one rule.
- **The spike viewer keeps what the product dropped.** None; a record.
  - It still offers Time, Time+gaps, the `optimized` order and the crossing-gap toggle, and it draws the branch-off side exit (Ralph: record the top-centre exit, do not update the viewer).
  - Its screenshots and `tools/lane-metrics.mjs`'s drawn crossing counts are side-exit numbers.
- **The design export does not show the deviations.** None; a record. They live in epics.md's Epic 10 header, not in `JJ Desktop Client.html`; a later design revision may adopt them.

## Surfaced 2026-09-28 while folding the design update into Epic 10: commit stacks and hover callouts (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph re-downloaded the Claude Design export (`a399daf`). It adds a callout beside a hovered card, hover-only quick actions, the bookmark popover beside the card, and commit stacks: linear runs collapse into one card with a popover, and a toolbar switch turns the grouping on and off. Ralph decided that a stack toggle never moves a column (the design re-packs the lanes) while the graph may compact vertically, that the callout's stats row is not planned, and that the design is inspiration rather than a 1:1 template. The stories were updated the same day: the Epic 10 header ("Design update 2026-09-28", prototype defects 17–20, the amended "Design status" house rule) and the Epic List entries of Epics 4 and 10; new Stories 4.13 and 10.17; dated notes in 4.10, 4.11, 10.1, 10.3, 10.5, 10.6, 10.7, 10.10, 10.11, 10.12, 10.15 and 10.16; PRD FR-GR-1. The items below fall outside those stories:_

- **The callout's stats row (A/M/D file counts, +/− lines).** Not planned, maybe never. Ralph: not that important for now, maybe we just don't build it.
  - File counts need a tree diff per commit. `show()`'s `fileChanges` (Story 9.6) has them, but `show()` snapshots on every call, which is too expensive per hover; an op-pinned summary call would be new backend work.

  - **Follow-up:** only if Ralph asks. Then a backend story for an op-pinned diff summary, and 10.6's callout gains the row.

SPEC §7.2's expected results (π = [0, 2, 3, 1]; free rows 209 / 377 with 4 lanes), Epic 10's prototype defect 15 ("13 of the 22 commits") and 10.1 item 13's CLI-faithful variant were all derived on the 22-commit sample.

Another export converts with `--rev <commit>` (SPEC §7.2's dated note).

The prototype does not use `stacked-on` yet.

## Surfaced in Story 4.10 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-09-30)

_The spec (`spec-4-10-graph-topology-content-split-op-pinned.md`) carries the first three items in its `deferred:` list (two decisions for Ralph and the gap the run left open); the fourth is a record. Numbers: its § Measurements (the bench's second run, 2026-10-01)._

- **Option (c)'s trigger is met at 10k packed — decision for Ralph.** Medium.
  - The decision (OQ 1 = (b)) says: adopt the per-handle timestamp cache when a first timestamped topology call exceeds ~50 ms on a packed store, or loose commits accumulate.
  - Measured: the FIRST `graph_topology(all(), with_timestamps)` call on a fresh handle is 53 ms (median) on `large-10k` packed, of which ~31 ms are the commit loads; 105 ms on `medium` and ~1.04 s on `large-10k` as committed (every object loose). A repeat call on a reused handle costs the same. Zanshin never packs, so a repo that only Zanshin writes accumulates loose commits.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether (c) (a commit id → timestamp cache on `zanshin_jj::Repo`, with its ADR-0002 Addendum clause and bound) or (d) (a Dart-side cache in the worker) is built now, or whether 10k packed at 53 ms stays acceptable. Nothing in 4.10 depends on it.
- **Memoising the pinned `ReadonlyRepo` — does its load dominate a page? Decision for Ralph.** Low.
  - The non-goal says: memoise the pinned repo within one operation id only if `load_operation` + `load_at` dominates a page.
  - Measured (empty-list call ÷ page, medians, cold commit cache, no changed-path index): the pinned load is 0.19 ms on `medium` loose, 0.86 ms on `large-10k` loose and 0.78 ms on `large-10k` packed — ~8 %, ~28 % and ~45 % of a 60-id page (~51 % packed with a changed-path index), and ~3 %, ~12 % and ~33 % of a 200-id page. So on a packed 10k store it is about half of a 60-id page.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether that dominates enough to memoise the pinned repo per operation id (handle state, an ADR-0002 Addendum clause) or whether 10.11 should page in larger batches instead. Nothing in 4.10 depends on it.
- **No real-repo numbers.** Low.
  - The bench measures the committed fixtures and a packed copy of `large-10k`; the optional local real repo needs a path (decisions-review-4-10-to-4-13.md, "Not done unattended").
  - **Follow-up:** run the bench against a copy of one of Ralph's repos if the fixture numbers are not convincing — never against the live repo, since the topology call snapshots.
- **A changed-path index has no measurable effect on the fixtures.** Low; a record.
  - Within noise: 5 samples per row, and several index rows are slower than their no-index rows. The fixtures' one octopus merge merges tiny trees, so `empty`'s tree merge is cheap there. The 0.78 ms per merge measured on the real repo (2026-09-26) is where the index would pay.
  - **Follow-up:** none; building the index is the user's `jj debug index-changed-paths`, not a Zanshin decision.

## Surfaced in Story 4.13 (bmad-build-auto, 2026-10-01)

_The spec (`spec-4-13-commit-stacks-layout-engine.md`) carries every item in its `deferred:` list. Numbers: its § Measurements; decisions: `decisions-review-4-10-to-4-13.md` ### 4.13._

- **D11: a split run's stack node leaves its parent edge unprotected below the card.** Low; a known limit, recorded in `docs/graph-layout.md` §4.8 and §9.
  - The stack reserves its column from its own card, so its parent edge (a branch-off from m_1's parent) rises in the stack's column without a reservation and may pass behind a lower card there. Reserving from one pitch above the parent (§4.3) would let collapsing grow `rows`. The 1,000-DAG invariants test produces 637 split runs (compact, greedy).
  - **Follow-up:** 10.4 / 10.17 decide whether the painter marks such an edge, or Ralph prefers a growing `rows` (a SPEC amendment + goldens).
- **The design sample's compaction with the `compact` order is measured, not derived by hand.** Low.
  - By hand (and asserted): `compact` mode, greedy, every run collapsed — all 19 → 15, default 9 → 8 (the planning estimate 9 → 7 missed a binding column constraint at sxmtvqno). With the `compact` order the engine gives 19 → 16 and 9 → 8; the stack goldens pin those outputs, but no hand derivation backs them.
  - **Follow-up:** none needed unless a review wants the derivation.
- **A group of equal Y with a long δ = 0 chain costs quadratic passes.** Low; a record.
  - §4.8's level sweep repeats passes over a group of equal Y until nothing changes; the implementations alternate the direction, so a chain of δ = 0 constraints (`free` nodes less than a minute apart with no edge between them, or (C)'s merge terms) settles in a few passes unless it runs against both directions. Not seen on any fixture or synthetic input (the `stacks` phase is 0.3–0.45 ms on fixture-large-all and ~0.8–1.0 ms on synth-10k, ~9–11 ms on synth-100k); an `assert` holds the bound in tests.
  - **Follow-up:** a worklist per group if a real repo shows it.
- **The stacks path has no all-inputs differential.** Low.
  - The product engine equals the spike's Dart on the committed stack goldens (the design sample × 3 requests × 2 modes × 2 orders, 60 seeded fuzz cases × 4), and both pass the invariants; `tools/compare.mjs --core` still compares only stack-less outputs (stacks have no Rust reference).
  - **Follow-up:** give `compare.mjs --core` a `--stacks` pass over `tools/stacks.mjs facts` inputs if the engine's stack code changes again.
- **No real-repo numbers for stacks.** Low.
  - Measured on the committed fixtures and the spike's synthetic inputs with synthetic facts (`node tools/stacks.mjs facts`); a real repo needs a path.
  - **Follow-up:** dump a copy with the spike's `rust-inputs`, add facts, bench with `graph_layout_cli bench --stacks`.
- ~~**`cargo make test` is red on `app/lib/src/shared/theme/zanshin_icons.dart` since `40a2a58` ("update icon font").**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-08:** fixed by Ralph's regenerated file in `c88a864` (SPDX header + `ignore_for_file: constant_identifier_names`); `test-dart-analyze` / `test-dart-format` (app) pass, and the full `cargo make test` is green again (verified during the jj-lib 0.46.0 bump). Original entry: Medium; needs Ralph (the story may not touch `app/lib`).
  - Both of the story's gate runs stop at `test-dart-analyze` (app): 103 info-level lints (`--fatal-infos`), all in that regenerated file; `test-dart-format` (app) reports the same file. The file is unchanged since the baseline `40a2a58`.

  - **Follow-up:** Ralph fixes or excludes the generated icons file (e.g. `dart fix --apply lib/src/shared/theme/zanshin_icons.dart` + `dart format` in `app/`, or an analyzer exclude for generated files); the gate is green again after that.

## Surfaced 2026-10-08 while bumping jj-lib 0.44.0 → 0.46.0 (console session, Ralph)

_The bump absorbed the API churn inside `zanshin-jj` and ported the CLI behavior the new API forces (per-workspace Git HEAD, `jj git import` without the HEAD import, the non-colocated conversion's `import_head_commit`, colocation detected through `open_git_repo_at_workdir`, `ResolvedRevset::evaluate` for `at_operation()`, `untracked_remote_tags()` in the default `immutable_heads()`). These CLI 0.45/0.46 behavior changes need a public-API decision and were left at the v0.44.0 behavior:_

- ~~**`undo` does not refuse a cross-workspace operation**~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-10-08 (Story 9.39, ported together with the undo stack):** `Repo::undo(allow_cross_workspace)`, `jj.undo_cross_workspace`, `jj.undo_workspace_removal`. Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_log.rs::undo`; CLI `cli/src/commands/undo.rs:120-157` @ v0.46.0] — since jj 0.46 `jj undo` refuses an operation performed in another workspace ("Refusing to undo operation … because it was performed in workspace …", hint `--allow-cross-workspace` / `jj op revert`), and warns when the undone operation is a `remove workspace`. Porting needs a new `allow_cross_workspace` parameter through `Repo::undo`, the FFI and `zanshin_core`, plus a new error code. Severity medium (Zanshin's undo can silently undo another workspace's work).
- ~~**`workspace root --name` still refuses an unreachable recorded path; `workspace list` still drops it**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-08 (console session): `workspace.rs::recorded_root` returns the store's path verbatim; `list` reports it, `resolve_root` returns `Warned<PathBuf>` and warns `jj.workspace_path_inaccessible` ("Cannot access workspace path: {path}") when the directory is gone; `WorkspaceError::PathUnresolved` / `jj.workspace_path_unresolved` removed (Rust, FFI, Dart, docs). Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/workspace.rs::resolve_recorded_root`; CLI `cli/src/commands/workspace/root.rs:42-66` @ v0.46.0, commit `d7e6ca981`] — jj 0.46 no longer canonicalizes: `root --name` prints the recorded path and only warns "Cannot access workspace path: …", and `list` shows every recorded path. Porting removes `WorkspaceError::PathUnresolved` / `jj.workspace_path_unresolved` and adds a warning kind (FFI + Dart). Severity low.
- ~~**`config set/unset/edit/path` without a path still answer `AmbiguousFile` when a scope has several files**~~ — RESOLVED 2026-10-08 (console session): `zanshin_jj::ConfigTarget` = `Scope(scope)` (the scope's FIRST file, else the new-file default) | `File(path)` (`--file`, ported `resolve_file_to_edit` incl. its create-forced repo/workspace check and `conf.d/*.toml`; a relative path joined onto the workspace root); `ConfigCmdError::AmbiguousFile` / `jj.config_ambiguous_file` replaced by `UnrecognizedFile` / `jj.config_unrecognized_file`; the wire keeps `scope` + `path` (exactly one, both/neither → `jj.invalid_input`); Dart `ConfigScopeTarget` / `ConfigFileTarget`. `config path` was never affected (its `ConfigLevelArgs` has no `--file`). Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/config_cmd.rs` (candidate selection); CLI `cli/src/commands/config/mod.rs:105-173` @ v0.46.0] — since jj 0.45 the CLI takes the FIRST loaded file of the scope instead of prompting, and `--file <PATH>` targets any recognized config file (a `conf.d/` file, a system file, `$JJ_CONFIG`). Severity low.
- **The graph walk's log set does not merge `at_operation()` indexes** — **MOVED 2026-10-08 → Story 9.42** (`9-42-graph-walk-at-operation-like-jj-log`, low priority; also covers `within_visibility` on the prioritize expression). Original entry: [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/graph.rs::LogParse::evaluate`] — `jj log` evaluates through `ResolvedRevset::evaluate` (`cli/src/commands/log.rs:161` @ v0.46.0), so a log revset with `at_operation(<sibling op>, …)` now works there; Zanshin evaluates the resolved expression against the repo's own index because the topology passes refine that expression, so such a revset still fails. Severity low.
- **Config migration rule not ported** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/config.rs` module docs] — the CLI's `default_config_migrations()` now rewrites `fix.tools.<name>.line-range-arg` → `line-range-args` (and warns); no Zanshin verb reads `fix.tools`. Port it with a `fix` verb. Severity none today.
- ~~**Workspace stories must adopt the 0.46 workspace model**~~ — DONE 2026-10-08: the 0.46 model is in the texts of Stories 9.13 (incl. the new `workspace remove`), 9.23 (incl. the `snapshot.auto-update-stale` recovery arm) and 9.21 (`redo --allow-cross-workspace`) in `epics.md` and `sprint-status.yaml`; `jj git colocation` for child workspaces became Story 9.45. Original entry: [Stories 9.13 / 9.23] — `jj workspace add --[no-]colocate` (+ `git.colocate`) creates a Git worktree for a child workspace, `forget` removes it, and `jj workspace remove` (new) deletes the directory; `remove_workspace` now also drops the workspace's Git HEAD. The colocation check already recognizes linked worktrees.
- **New CLI verbs since v0.44.0 recorded as stories** (2026-10-08, console session): `jj converge` (0.45) → Story 9.43 (product decision pending, Ralph); `jj file edit` / `jj file delete` (0.46) → Story 9.44; `jj git colocation status|enable|disable`, per workspace since 0.46 → Story 9.45; `jj git push` to several remotes + `revsets.git-push` (0.46) → Story 9.20's addendum; the 0.46 `finish` location and the new verbs → Story 9.40's update.
- **No full v0.44 → v0.46 parity audit of the other ported verbs yet** — **MOVED 2026-10-08 → Story 9.46** (`9-46-v044-v046-parity-audit-ported-verbs`, Ralph asked for a story; it lists the changed CLI sources per shipped verb). Original entry: the CLI changed `describe`, `status`, `log`, `file show/list/annotate/untrack`, `bookmark list/track/move/advance`, `git init/clone/remote add/remote list`, `op log/show/diff` between the tags (`git -C ../jj diff v0.44.0 v0.46.0 -- cli/src/commands`). The bump only covered what the compiler and the gate surfaced; source comments citing `@ v0.44.0` line numbers stay correct for that tag but need re-checking at v0.46.0 when touched.

## Surfaced 2026-10-08 while analysing "fork jj instead of re-porting the CLI" (console session, Ralph)

_The analysis and POC live on branch `poc/jj-cli-reuse` under `spikes/jj-cli-reuse/`. The differential parity oracle there (`poc/tests/parity_zanshin.rs`) runs the same operation through `zanshin-jj` and the stock jj CLI and compares repo state._

- **`describe` and `new` never apply `templates.commit_trailers`** [`crates/zanshin-jj/src/op_support.rs::apply_trailers` (called only from its inline tests); CLI `cli/src/commands/describe.rs:153-161` and `new.rs:234` @ v0.46.0, already present @ v0.44.0] — with `templates.commit_trailers = 'format_signed_off_by_trailer(self)'`, `jj describe -m T` stores `T` + a blank line + `Signed-off-by: …`, while Zanshin stores `T`. This predates 0.46. Found by reading the code; the parity oracle confirmed it for `describe`. `new` is the same pattern but was not run through the oracle. The not-yet-ported `commit`, `squash`, `split` and `workspace add` apply trailers too (`add_trailers`). No story covers it. Severity medium: a silent divergence on shipped verbs.

## Surfaced 2026-10-14 while folding the floating client and its design system into Epics 10 and 11 (console session, Ralph)

_Ralph re-downloaded the Claude Design export (`e74ced3`): the reference is now `JJ Desktop Client Floating.html`, and a new design-system project sits in `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/design_system/` (synced through #9). He decided that the export wins where it explicitly changed a recorded decision, that motion follows the client's FLIP model, that the inspector, the card menu, the Bookmarks rail, the search and the Configurations dialog are built to the design, and that Claude now builds the whole Flutter UI (new Epic 11; Story 11.0 recorded the scope change). The stories were updated the same day: the Epic 10 header ("Design update 2026-10-14", decisions (a)–(g), prototype defects 25–76, amendments to defects 2, 8, 16, 17 and 21–23, the house rules Theme and Design-system sync), new Stories 4.14, 4.15, 9.48–9.50, 10.21–10.27 and 11.0–11.10, the refined 9.25, and dated notes in 4.11, 4.13, 5.1, 5.6, 9.15–9.17, 9.19, 9.31 and most Epic 10 stories. Six older entries above are marked as moved, moot or superseded. The items below fall outside those stories:_

### Graph layout and lines

- **The spike's design-input tool cannot read `e74ced3`.** Low: no recording needs it.
  - `node tools/design-input.mjs --rev e74ced3` throws "no top-level declaration of G". It extracts six declarations by name from `graph-view.jsx` (design-input.mjs:33-54), which now imports them from `graph-layout.jsx` (GV:6-7).
  - GD and the gap walk are unchanged (GL:435-460 = GV@e243dd2:145-170), so the `e243dd2` inputs stand for this export too.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.14 and 4.15 add a loader that evaluates `graph-layout.jsx` whole. It is plain JS: stub `revsetMatch` as LSH:54 does, and stub `placeGapMarkers` or extract it.
- **The joint column packing may widen the graph.** Medium: unmeasured.
  - Chains may share a lane only when they are apart in both layouts, so 4.14's packing can need more columns than either per-mode packing.
  - The export compensates with an exact chain colouring for small graphs and with its whole-lane optimizer (GL:492-568). 4.14 ports neither.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.14's measurements on the real 1.4k-commit repo and the scale inputs. If the width grows noticeably, port the exact colouring for small graphs.
- **Router B's cost on the UI isolate is unknown.** Medium.
  - The geometry stage runs per layout and per card size, and track assignment is quadratic in a cluster's size (GL:87-112).
  - **Follow-up:** 4.15's timings at large-10k and synth-100k set the `Isolate.run` threshold (its item 4).
- **Free detours use a unit threshold.** Low.
  - The export's pixel test leaves room from 13 units at H 62 and from 12 at H 40 (GL:162-172). 4.15's unit rule (12) detours a little more often with compact cards.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.15's oracle lists the cases; tune the rule if they show.
- **Day dividers can cross cards around midnight.** Low; accepted in 10.2.
  - Commits on either side of midnight less than about 25 min apart (about 11 min with compact cards) overlap vertically. So SYNC #8's "the band around a divider never holds cards" fails there (GL:597-602; prototype defect 45).
  - **Follow-up:** none, unless real repos show it often.
- **Equal timestamps sort differently from the export.** Low; a record.
  - A rebase gives many commits the same committer time. GL orders ties by generation depth, priority and design index (GL:395); the engine orders them by index (D3; LE:324-329). Both keep parents first.
  - Only unrelated commits with identical timestamps can end up at different heights.
  - **Follow-up:** none.
- **Gutter overflow.** Low.
  - More than 9 lines on one gutter × step ±6 px each into the neighbouring cards (GL:193-197).
  - **Follow-up:** 4.15 reports it. Widen the gutter's budget only if real repos show it.

### Cards, motion and the minimap

- **Compact's minimap labels need a day per node.** Medium.
  - The design labels a Compact row with the local calendar day of its newest commit, shown only when that day is older than the row above's (GV:438-449).
  - 4.11 emits day buckets only for Free (4.11 open question 9), and the view never computes days.
  - **Follow-up:** 4.11 (or 4.14) emits a local day key per node in both modes; 10.16 labels Compact rows from it.
- **The Animation Show Case is superseded.** None; a record.
  - AS stays in the export as a playground.
  - Its values are no longer planned (decision (c)): the 250 ms stack slide, the 1.8 px/ms tracing, the 600 ms leave with a 16 px blur and a 16 px rise.
  - **Follow-up:** none. 10.20's demo host keeps the slow-motion switch.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the demo host becomes the Widgetbook "Graph" folder, and the slow-motion switch is Widgetbook's TimeDilation addon (decision 16.30; Stories 10.1, 10.20, 11.11).)*
- **The design system's id-length rule disagrees with its card.** Low; a record.
  - DS readme.md:65 says change ids have 5 characters on cards.
  - DS ChangeCard and the client show 8 on detailed non-@ cards, and 5 on compact cards and on @ (DS components/graph/ChangeCard.jsx:25; GV:278).
  - **Follow-up:** the next design-system sync. 10.3 keeps 8/5.
- **The design system's legend chip is wider than the decided pill.** Low; a record.
  - DS components/graph/graph.css:22 gives the card's chip `max-width: calc(100% − 20px)`.
  - Decision (g) keeps W/2 − 34 px, painted above the edges (prototype defect 26).
  - **Follow-up:** mention it at the next design-system sync.
- **Nothing shows or sets the horizontal position.** Low.
  - The graph has no scrollbars (FC:18-19), and the minimap moves only vertically (GV:496-499).
  - Only a pan and the @ pill's left or right arrow reach a far column.
  - **Follow-up:** watch it on wide repos in the demo host; add a horizontal hint only if Ralph asks.

### Search

- **The search matches text over every change.** None; a record.
  - 10.21's Change scope and Any's Changes group match subjects and descriptions across all changes (SR:94-126), while 10.11 loads content only for the visible range.
  - **Follow-up:** covered: 9.25 item 2 (`find_changes` over `description(substring-i:)` / `subject()`) is the backend path.
- **Path suggestions see only `@`'s files.** Low.
  - The design gives every mock commit the inspector's changed paths and indexes the paths and folders of all commits (SR:14-20, :94-107). So it also finds deleted and historical paths.
  - 10.21 lists the files of `@`, as the CLI's `jj log` completion does (`cli/src/complete.rs:1143-1152` @ v0.46.0). A typed path or glob still filters the whole history.
  - **Follow-up:** only if it is missed: a backend listing of changed paths over jj-lib's changed-path index (`src/default_index/changed_path.rs`; `files()` already reads it, `revset_engine.rs:1361-1371`).
- **Author suggestions come only from loaded content.** Low.
  - The design lists every author of the repository (SR:96-98, :131-137). Neither Zanshin nor the CLI has an author list.
  - "Authors containing “x”" and typed patterns still reach every author.
  - **Follow-up:** only if it is missed: an op-pinned distinct-author read. It loads every commit, like 4.10's timestamps.
- **Counts on large repositories are unmeasured.** Medium.
  - Without a changed-path index, a single `files()` count diffs every commit. At 100k it can hold the serial worker for seconds and delay a reload queued behind it.
  - The cap bounds dense matches only.
  - **Follow-up:** measure 9.25's counts on large-10k (loose and packed) and on the synthetic inputs once 9.25 lands. If they are too slow: count File rows only when they are highlighted, or rely on the changed-path index where the repo has one.
- **Global configurations name one repository's paths and people.** Low.
  - Decision (g) keeps configurations and the default search global, as designed (RC:23, :40). A `files()` or author rule names one repository; in another it matches nothing.
  - A configuration chosen as the default search then opens other repositories on an empty graph, and ✕ leads back to it.
  - **Follow-up:** revisit after use. The options: per-repository configurations, or a default search per repository.
- **No raw revset in the floating client.** Low; deferred (decision (g), Claude's default).
  - The six rule functions cannot express `trunk()..@`, `bookmarks()`, `conflicts()` or a user's own `[revset-aliases]`. Only `revsets.log`, set outside Zanshin, reaches such a revset (as the `jj log` built-in).
  - **Follow-up:** 10.22's design question (b). If it is built, 9.25's `NoSuchFunction` candidates gain a consumer.
- **Design debris around the search.** None; a record for citations.
  - FC:68-72, :301 and :303 still style the retired `.gtb-select` inside the capsule.
  - AF:5 still describes SYNC #5's variant segment.
  - RC:2 and RC:116 describe the narrowing model that #6 removed.
  - The docked client (`JJ Desktop Client.html`) keeps a `RevsetSelect` with configurations and the docked dialog shell (RC:129-180). It is not the reference (`design/CLAUDE.md:3`).
  - **Follow-up:** none; a later export may clean them up.

### Actions, drag-and-drop and the toast

- **No non-drag path for rebase, bookmark moves and hunk moves.** Medium (accessibility).
  - The design moves changes, bookmarks, files and hunks only by drag, and the panel's "Rebase…" button is gone, so keyboard and VoiceOver users cannot rebase (10.10, 10.27, 10.15; GV:792-930; IN:193-206).
  - Deferred by decision (g). The bookmark popover's "Move to…" (10.15) is the one non-drag move.
  - **Follow-up:** a later story once Ralph wants it.
- **⌘Z works only while a toast is visible.** Low.
  - An app-wide Edit › Undo and Redo (⇧⌘Z = `jj redo`, 9.21) is not designed (GV:757-762; 10.23).
  - **Follow-up:** with 9.21, if Ralph wants a menu-bar Undo.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: undo has three places (decision 16.7): the toast's Undo; ⌘Z and Edit › "Undo ‹action›" for Zanshin's own latest operation while it is still current, with ⇧⌘Z = `jj redo`; the Undo Timeline for older operations. Every undo ends in the toast "Undid ‹action› · Redo" (decision 16.46). The menu bar is the new Story 11.13; Redo needs 9.21.)*
- **The DS UI kit deviates from the client on drag-and-drop.** None; a record.
  - The kit rests 520 ms before the menu (the client: 260 ms), places the menu below the card first (the client: above), and binds ⌘Z always (the client: only while a toast is visible) (DS ui_kits/desktop/app.jsx:272, :305; GV:819, :842, :757-762).
  - **Follow-up:** the client is the reference; raise it at the next design-system sync.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16 for ⌘Z: the spec is now decision 16.7 (DS components/overlays/Toast.prompt.md:14), so neither the client's "only while a toast is visible" nor the kit's "always" holds; 11.13 builds it. The drop menu keeps 260 ms (decision (a4); the DS's `--delay-drop-menu` 520 is not adopted). The kit itself is still wrong; the 2026-10-16 section below records it among the DS leftovers.)*

### The inspector and the diff

- **Viewed state needs a per-repository store.** Low.
  - The design keeps "viewed" in `localStorage` per change id and path (SH:56-76). Decision (g) keys it per repository by (change id, path, diff fingerprint), and no store holds that kind of per-repository UI state yet.
  - **Follow-up:** 10.24 picks the store, next to 10.17's per-repository stack toggles (Claude's proposal).
- **jj prints no hunk-header symbol for Dart.** Low.
  - The design shows a symbol on Dart hunks (DF:14, :26). jj's symbol patterns have no Dart (`../jj` v0.46.0 `cli/src/source_symbol.rs:57-93`), so CLI parity shows none.
  - **Follow-up:** none in Zanshin: a Zanshin-only pattern would diverge from the CLI, and an upstream pattern arrives with a pin bump.
- **Line-level selection inside a hunk.** Low.
  - jj's builtin editor toggles single lines (scm-record); the design selects whole hunks. 9.50's ranges could express single lines.
  - **Follow-up:** a later story, if Ralph wants it.
- **Conflicted files have no designed diff view.** Low.
  - `jj diff` materializes conflict markers into the diff text (`cli/src/diff_util.rs:1884`); the design shows only the "Conflict" flag (IN:233).
  - **Follow-up:** 10.24 shows jj's text; a designed conflict view comes with 9.17's `resolve`.
- **Word-level and syntax marks have no consumer story.** Low.
  - With line-level first (decision (g)), 5.2–5.4 come later, and no Epic 10 story renders segments.
  - **Follow-up:** a follow-up story to 10.24 when 5.2–5.4 are scheduled.
- **No commit affordance on an empty @.** Low.
  - The composer needs files on @ (IN:177). `jj commit -m` on an empty @ is `describe` plus `new` in one operation (`cli/src/commands/commit.rs:35-36`).
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides whether 10.25 shows the composer on an empty @ (Claude's proposal: no; describe and "New change" cover it, in two operations).
- **9.47's ledger row `describe_gui_text` retires with 10.8's Save.** Low.
  - The row exists because the GUI was assumed to send the text without the CLI's trailing newline (`crates/zanshin-parity/tests/oracle/ledger.rs:71-78`).
  - **Follow-up:** when 10.8 lands, its scenario sends "S\n\nD\n" and the row goes.
- **A hunk selection's staleness under constant snapshots.** Medium.
  - Every verb snapshots @ first (9.26), so an editor that autosaves changes @ between selecting hunks and running the composer. If staleness were judged by @'s commit id, commits would keep failing.
  - **Follow-up:** 9.50 judges staleness by the left side, the revision's parent tree (Claude's proposal); confirm it in 9.50's spec.
- **The combined description must be known before squash runs.** Medium.
  - The CLI decides it inside the command and opens an editor (`cli/src/commands/squash.rs:363-396`); the GUI has to ask first (10.25, 10.27).
  - **Follow-up:** 9.15's plan phase returns the combined text when needed (its note); 9.31's `diff` source can carry the flag.
- **10.8's and 10.9's `handle.dart` citations are stale.** Low.
  - 10.8 cites `handle.dart:690` for `show`; 10.9 cites :228, :242, :257, :271, :284, :861 and :891. The file is `packages/zanshin_core/lib/src/worker/handle.dart`: describe :344, newChange :358, edit :373, abandon :387, undo :407, show :813, bookmarkCreate :986, bookmarkDelete :1016.
  - **Follow-up:** re-point them when each story is refined.

### The floating chrome and the shell

- **Workstreams and Reviews have no backend.** Low; a record.
  - The export's rail lists both (AF:22-23), and its empty state names `jj workstream new` (AF:124). jj v0.46.0 has no `workstream` command, and reviews exist only as `jj gerrit` (`cli/src/commands/` at v0.46.0).
  - 11.3 hides both items (decision (g)).
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides what each should mean, for example Gerrit or forge reviews. Backend stories follow, and then 11.3 shows the items.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16 for Workstreams: they are dropped everywhere, not hidden, because they are not a jj concept (decision 16.26; 11.3's rail, 10.14, and 11.10 removes `ZI.workstream` and its SVG). Reviews stays hidden until a backend exists; this entry stays its record.)*
- **Remote management has no surface.** Low.
  - 9.5's `git remote add`, `remove`, `rename` and `set-url` (done) have no UI consumer.
  - The Projects dialog's Remotes tab is inert (SH:2067), and 11.6 proposes to drop it. 11.7's Project tab only picks the fetch and push remotes.
  - **Follow-up:** a "Remotes" group in 11.7's Project tab (add, rename, remove, set URL), when Ralph wants it.
- **Bookmark tracking has no surface.** Low.
  - 9.29's untracked rows and 9.11's `track` and `untrack` (backlog) are outside 10.14 (its non-goals).
  - **Follow-up:** a row action in 10.14's panel, through 10.15 and 10.9, once 9.11 and 9.29 land.
- **Raw config editing has no surface.** Low.
  - 9.28's `configFileRead` and `configFileWrite` (done), the GUI split of `jj config edit`, have no consumer. 11.7 edits only its own rows.
  - **Follow-up:** an "Open config file…" link per tab, if Ralph wants one.
- **The docked client's command palette and help button are gone.** None; a record.
  - The old status bar advertised a ⌘K palette, and the window had a "?" button (APP@e243dd2:972, :2492).
  - The floating client has neither, and no story plans them.

### The design system

- **macOS never reports Reduce motion to Flutter.** Medium: every reduce-motion rule in Epic 10 (10.6 item 12, 10.17, 10.19, 10.20) and the splash (`app/lib/src/features/bootstrap/splash_card.dart:54`) is inert on macOS today.
  - Only the iOS embedder sets the flag (`engine/src/flutter/shell/platform/darwin/ios/framework/Source/AccessibilityFeatures.swift`). Nothing under `darwin/macos` or `darwin/common` calls `FlutterEngineUpdateAccessibilityFeatures` (checked in the local SDK checkout `flutter-3.47-candidate.0`, 2026-10-14).
  - Reduce transparency has no Flutter flag at all.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.1 item 7 feeds `MediaQuery.disableAnimations` from `zanshin_shell`, and 11.2 reads `reduceTransparency` from the same place. An upstream Flutter issue only if Ralph wants one.
- **The DS icon files lost `currentColor`.** Low.
  - 46 of the 48 SVGs in `DS assets/icons/ui/`, and all 46 in `design/icons/ui/`, stroke with `#000` and embed a C2PA manifest of about 7.8 KB. Only `change.svg` and `description.svg` keep `currentColor`.
  - `DS components/brand/Icon.jsx:4-72` holds the same geometry with `currentColor`. `ZanshinIcon`'s srcIn tint hides the difference; an icon-font generator or an untinted `SvgPicture` would not.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.1's icon rule copies from `Icon.jsx`. Ask Claude Design to keep `currentColor` only if Ralph wants the files themselves fixed.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: in the DS downloaded 2026-10-16 every glyph is a file with `currentColor`, 58 of them (DS readme.md:344, :350). The app copies the files themselves, stripped of `<metadata>` and `xmlns:c2pa`, into a precompiled SVG pipeline with an `IconRegistry`, and the gate checks their bytes (decisions 16.6, 16.32, 16.33; 11.1 item 11 as amended 2026-10-16).)*
- **Some component values have no token.** Low.
  - `DS readme.md:90` lists radius 13 for glass menus, and `DS components/menus/menus.css:18`, `:36` use it, but `DS tokens/effects.css:5-17` has no such token.
  - The radii 4.5, 5 and 7, the glass field well rgba(8,10,22,.45) and the gold primary's mixes are literals too (`DS components/forms/forms.css:31`, `:38`, `:45-46`; `DS components/graph/graph.css:51`, `:97`; `DS components/actions/actions.css:8-12`).
  - **Follow-up:** 11.1 names them in the token layer, marked as untokenised; the parity test cannot cover them. A DS sync request if Ralph wants them tokenised.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the DS's design review tokenised them: one radius scale with `--radius-tag` 5, `--radius-chip` 7, `--radius-well` 12 and `--radius-popover` 13 (DS readme.md:321); every mix a control uses is a fixed token, such as `--primary-action` (DS readme.md:298); literal colours became tokens such as `--seg-inset-bg` and `--field-bg` (DS readme.md:286, part C). 11.1's 2026-10-16 note takes them as tokens.)*
- **The dialog entrance differs between the DS and the client.** Low.
  - The DS's `Dialog` enters with `zs-pop` in 160 ms (`DS components/surfaces/surfaces.css:28`).
  - The client's Settings and Configurations dialogs use `gset-in` in 240 ms (`STC:28`, `FC:271`), and so does the DS's own `RevsetDialog` (`DS components/search/search.css:99`). There is no duration token for it.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.2 uses 240 ms (Claude's proposal). The next DS sync can align `Dialog`.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the DS unified the entrance to `--dur-dialog` 220 ms for Dialog, Settings and Configurations (DS readme.md:302; DS tokens/motion.css:32), and 11.2 uses 220 (its 2026-10-16 note). The only sheets left are ConfirmDialog and Push (decision 16.17); Settings, Projects and Configurations are windows now, with no entrance (decisions 16.13, 16.19; DS readme.md:90).)*
- **The dot grid survives in the DS.** None; a record.
  - `--grid-dot` (`DS tokens/colors.css:38`, `:70`), `--grid-step` (`DS tokens/spacing.css:19`) and `.zs-dots` (`DS components/base.css:7`) remain, although the floating client dropped the grid (`FC:20`; `DS readme.md:84`).
  - **Follow-up:** the token layer carries them for the parity test only; nothing paints them.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the DS removed `--grid-dot`, `--grid-step` and `.zs-dots` (DS readme.md:327).)*
- **The shadow names are crossed.** Low.
  - `--shadow-popover` is the context menu's shadow (`DS components/menus/menus.css:2`), while `--shadow-menu` belongs to popovers and glass menus (`DS components/surfaces/surfaces.css:10`; `DS components/menus/menus.css:36`).
  - `DS guidelines/elevation.html:11` labels them that way; `DS readme.md:94` calls the latter "menus".
  - **Follow-up:** 11.1 names the Flutter shadows by role (context menu, popover, callout, dialog) and keeps the CSS name beside each.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: one menu material for every menu, popover, callout and the peek: 13 px on `--fl-sheen` over `--fl-bg-solid`, 1 px `--fl-border`, `--shadow-menu`; `--shadow-popover`, `--shadow-callout` and `--bg-elevated` are retired (DS readme.md:90, :284, :322). 11.2 builds it (its 2026-10-16 note).)*
- **`nami_loader_pulse.json` is not in the app.** None; a record.
  - `DS assets/motion/` and `design/lottie/` have it. `app/assets/lottie/` has the other two loaders, byte-identical to the DS's.
  - **Follow-up:** the story that first shows the pulse loader copies it.
- **The app icon is an older Nami variant.** Low.
  - The app uses `app/macos/Runner/NamiIndigo.icon` (`ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_APPICON_NAME = NamiIndigo` in `app/macos/Runner.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`). It has one `waves.svg` layer on a Display P3 gradient, with translucency .5.
  - The DS's bundle has three wave layers, #f4cf80, #dca85b and #b58346, on #1d2540 → #131831 (`DS assets/app-icon/README.txt`). Its `icon.json` uses a hand-written schema (angle and stops), not Icon Composer's, so it cannot be dropped in as it is.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.10 item 6 re-authors the bundle with the three layers in Icon Composer's format. Ralph checks it in the Dock.
- **DS prompt examples show retired parts.** Low.
  - `DS components/surfaces/Island.prompt.md:5` still uses `CommandField`, retired in syncs #5 and #6.
  - `DS components/surfaces/Popover.prompt.md:6` offers the Layout "Compact, Time"; Time is gone since #7.
  - **Follow-up:** mention it with the next DS sync. Stories cite the components' code, not these examples.
  - *(Resolved 2026-10-16: the DS fixed `Island.prompt.md` (GraphSearch, not CommandField) and `Popover.prompt.md` (Free · Compact, not Time) (DS readme.md:303).)*
- **Density has no tokens.** Low.

_Ralph re-downloaded the design-system project twice (rounds 2 and 3; the DS readme still says "Synced through #9") and validated each round against the stories (V1 = `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/research/design-system-validation-2026-10-15.md`, V2 = `…-round-2-2026-10-16.md`, V3 = `…-round-3-2026-10-16.md`; RC = `technical-ui-controls-yaru-material-custom-and-macos-accents-research-2026-10-15.md`). On 2026-10-16 he accepted every recommendation of the three reports, Claude's proposals included ("mit deinen Vorschlägen"). The stories were updated the same day: the decisions 16.1–16.47 in the Epic 11 header, a "Design update 2026-10-16" in the Epic List and the Epic 10 header (with the new house rule Commands), new Stories 11.11 (Zs control library, Widgetbook catalogue, Marionette), 11.12 (window foundation) and 11.13 (menu bar and shortcuts), and dated notes in 9.20, 9.21, 9.23, 9.32, most Epic 10 stories and 11.1–11.10. `DS <path>:<line>` below refers to the DS downloaded 2026-10-16 (round 3). Ten entries of the 2026-10-14 section and one older entry above are marked resolved. The items below fall outside those stories:_

### Declined or deferred by Ralph

- **A Workspaces section in the Project menu and a `<name>@` go-to.** None; declined by Ralph on 2026-10-15 (decision 16.27; round-2 prompt header item 4; V2 §6).
  - Workspaces stay visible in two places without a new surface: `name@` on cards (10.3, decision 16.28) and the workspace name in Undo Timeline rows (11.9 item 5).
- **Reads with `--ignore-working-copy`.** None; declined (CLI parity, decision 16.28).
  - While the working copy is stale only the no-snapshot reads work (`commit_contents`, `log_defaults`, `workspace_root`); the way out is 9.23's `update-stale`.
- **Patrol and Patrol MCP.** Low; deferred (decision 16.31).
  - **Follow-up — revisit trigger:** Patrol's `$.platform.macos` ships AND its binding works with a real windowing owner (several views). Until then Marionette 0.7.0 plus the CGWindowList probe cover it.

### Windows and the shell

- **Configurations floats over Settings and Projects too.** Low; watch (decision 16.42 (b), Claude's proposal; 11.12 item 6).
  - The `revset-configs` window takes `.floating` while Zanshin is active, so it also sits above Settings and Projects.
  - **Follow-up:** if that annoys in use, float it only above the project window, for example by lowering it while Settings or Projects is key (Claude's proposal).
- **Blur-triggered settings writes fire on every window switch.** Low (11.7, 11.12 item 9; r3out windows.md C7).
  - Every window switch blurs the focused text field, so 11.7's write-on-blur rows write each time the user moves to another Zanshin window.
  - **Follow-up:** check it in 11.7; write only when the value changed (Claude's proposal).
- **Settings' top-anchored resize needs native work.** Medium (11.12 item 3; r4out windows W-6).
  - `setContentSize` keeps the bottom-left origin, so a Settings window whose height follows its tab moves its top edge. The runner has to re-anchor the top.
  - **Follow-up:** 11.12 builds it; if it flickers, a fixed height per tab is the fallback (Claude's proposal).
- **Several workspaces of one repository in the project registry.** Low; open (r3out workstream.md critique 2, MISSED).
  - The registry (11.5) is keyed by path. With no Workspaces section (decision 16.27) a user can still add a sibling workspace's folder. Whether it is its own entry or a child of its repository is not decided.
  - **Follow-up:** Ralph decides when 11.5 is refined.

### Tooling: Widgetbook, Marionette, icons

- **Widgetbook's dev plugins reach the release build.** Low (11.11; decision 16.29).
  - Widgetbook's dev-only plugins (`url_launcher_macos`) end up in release builds (flutter#163874), and adding it changes the app's `GeneratedPluginRegistrant`. Catalogue builds also run the `zanshin_core` build hook.
  - **Follow-up:** open. Once 11.11 adds Widgetbook, check whether `url_launcher_macos` ships in the release bundle, and keep it out if it does (Claude's proposal; 11.11 does not cover it).
  - **2026-10-18, Story 11.11:** checked — it ships: Widgetbook adds `url_launcher_macos` to `app/macos/Flutter/GeneratedPluginRegistrant.swift` and the generated SwiftPM plugin package, which serve every build mode, so it links into a release `Zanshin.app` (flutter_tools filters dev plugins only on Android, Linux and Windows; flutter#163874) (commit ff1ccc6); left: Ralph decides how to keep it out — accept it until flutter#163874 lands (registered, never called), a pure-Dart stub override of `url_launcher_macos` in the workspace root, or the catalogue as its own app package (against 11.11's non-goal).
- **Marionette's limits.** Low (decision 16.31; r3out tooling.md).
  - Coordinate gestures still hit the first view; screenshots are "all views, in attach order", unlabeled and Flutter-layer only; bounds carry no view id. Hence the rule: match by Semantics identifier or text, never by coordinates, and scope listings with `ancestor_keys` on keyed window roots.
  - **Follow-up:** retire the marionette fork branch `fix/multi-view-gestures` once `marionette_flutter` ^0.7.0 is in (11.11 non-goal).
- **The IconRegistry needs a hot restart.** Low (decision 16.32).
  - The registry loads the precompiled icons once at boot, so a changed icon shows only after a hot restart.
  - **Follow-up:** measure the paint cost of `ZanshinIcon`'s saveLayer per icon on a large graph; batch or cache only if it shows in frame times (Claude's proposal).

  - `packages/zanshin_shell/lib/src/window_drag_area.dart:3` imports `package:flutter/material.dart`, against the house rule that Material types come from `package:material_ui` only; 11.12 touches that package.
